<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Assuming the stated deterministic critical-reference estimates, we prove that the relative second moment of the quenched critical spin correlation in the square-lattice Ising model with independent fair bonds $`1\pm\varepsilon`$ grows as $`(\log r)^{1/4+o(1)}`$ for each sufficiently small fixed <i>ε</i> &gt; 0. The correlation is evaluated at the physical critical temperature, with the thermodynamic limit taken before the disorder moments and the large-distance limit.

- **claim-main-quenched-exponent** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Assume the deterministic reference-model estimates of [[ReferenceIsing](#ref-ReferenceIsing)], at the scopes stated in Section <a href="#sec:reference" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reference">2</a>. There is $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that for every fixed $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_0)$, $$\label{eq:main-limit} \lim_{r\to\infty,\,r\in\mathbb N} \frac{\log R_\varepsilon(r)}{\log\log r}=\frac14.$$ The critical inverse temperature in <a href="#eq:quenched-correlation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quenched-correlation">[eq:quenched-correlation]</a> is the physical value <a href="#eq:critical-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-definition">[eq:critical-definition]</a>; it is the unique positive solution of $$\label{eq:critical-curve} \sinh\bigl(2\beta(1-\varepsilon)\bigr) \sinh\bigl(2\beta(1+\varepsilon)\bigr)=1.$$
  - Explanation: Granting the deterministic reference-model estimates, the logarithm of the relative second moment of the quenched critical correlation, divided by the double logarithm of the distance, tends to one quarter for every sufficiently small fixed disorder strength. The critical inverse temperature used is the physical one, characterised by the self-dual equation.
- **claim-invariant-charges** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ref-charges)): There are linear functionals $\ell$ on bounded local even functions modulo constants and $\ell_s$ on bounded local odd functions with $$\ell(e(z))=1,\qquad \ell_s(\sigma_z)=1.$$ They commute with translations and square symmetries and are invariant under reference conditional averaging whenever the cut encloses the support. For $b=1$ write $\ell_b=\ell$, and for $b=d$ write $\ell_b=\ell_s$; the subscript in $\ell_s$ means spin. If $F$ has the corresponding parity and support in a square of radius $w$ above a fixed threshold, then $$\label{ref:charge-bound} \|\ell_b(F)\|\le Cw^b\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty, \qquad \ell_b(F)=\kappa_b^{-1}\lim_{R\to\infty}R^b d_R(F).$$ The disk in this limit may be centered at any fixed lattice point. In the even case $\ell_b$ annihilates constants.
  - Explanation: Linear functionals on even and odd local functions exist that take value one on the energy and spin fields, commute with lattice symmetries, and are unchanged by reference conditional averaging around their support.
- **claim-averaging-charge-cancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-ref-operator)): Fix $\nu>0$ smaller than $p-b$ in the applicable row of <a href="#ref:exponents" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ref:exponents">[ref:exponents]</a>. For all sufficiently large $w$ and $R\ge Cw$, a function $F$ of that parity, supported in $B_w(z)$, satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \left\lVert P_{R,z}F^\circ\right\rVert_\infty &\le C(w/R)^b\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty,\label{ref:ordinary-decay}\\ \left\lVert P_{R,z}F^\circ-\ell_b(F)P_{R,z}\mathcal O_b(z)\right\rVert_\infty &\le C(w/R)^{p-\nu}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty. \label{ref:improved-decay} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, the second bound is the improved decay bound for a function of charge zero. We shall use $\nu=1/4$. The exponent $3-\nu$ requires the individual even function to be invariant under right-angle rotations; invariance of its probability law alone does not suffice. For a site at fixed positive relative distance from the cutting contour, $$\label{ref:microscopic-decay} \left\lVert P_{R,z}e(y)\right\rVert_\infty\le CR^{-1},\qquad \left\lVert P_{R,z}\sigma_y\right\rVert_\infty\le CR^{-d}.$$ The first bound also holds for a single plane-centered bond product.
  - Explanation: Conditional averaging of a localised function decays at the ordinary rate, and at an improved rate once its invariant charge times the field line is subtracted.
- **claim-energy-spin-products** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ref-ope)): Uniformly in the direction of $z\in\mathbb Z^2$ as $\|z\|\to\infty$, $$\begin{aligned} \langle e(z)e(0)\rangle_0 &=\frac{a^2+o(1)}{\|z\|^2},\label{ref:ee-correlation}\\ \ell\bigl(e(z)e(0)\bigr)&=o(\|z\|^{-1}),\label{ref:ee-charge}\\ \ell_s\bigl(e(z)\sigma_0\bigr)^2 &=\frac{a^2+o(1)}{4\|z\|^2}.\label{ref:es-charge} \end{aligned}$$ The even charge in <a href="#ref:ee-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ref:ee-charge">[ref:ee-charge]</a> is evaluated modulo the plane mean. In addition to Proposition <a href="#ref:operator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ref:operator">3</a>, the proof uses only separated lattice limits and the continuum expansions <a href="#ref:continuum-ope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ref:continuum-ope">[ref:continuum-ope]</a>.
  - Explanation: Uniformly in direction, the lattice energy two-point function has the expected inverse-square decay, the even product charge is of smaller order, and the squared energy-spin charge has a precise coefficient.
- **claim-buffered-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-ref-comparison)): Consider a finite critical reference graph in a square chart. Let $I,J$ and $V_{\mathrm{pin}}$ be pairwise disjoint vertex sets, and prescribe common spin values on $V_{\mathrm{pin}}$. Suppose $I\subset B_w(z)$ and $J\subset B_R(z)^c$. For $R\ge(1+c)w$ with fixed $c>0$, every assignment $a$ on $I$ and assignments $\tau,\tau'$ on $J$ satisfy $$\label{ref:density-ratio} C_c^{-1}\le \frac{\mathbb P_0(\sigma_I=a\mid\sigma_J=\tau,\sigma_{V_{\mathrm{pin}}})} {\mathbb P_0(\sigma_I=a\mid\sigma_J=\tau',\sigma_{V_{\mathrm{pin}}})} \le C_c.$$ For $R\ge8w\ge8$, the absolute logarithm of this ratio is at most $C(w/R)^\theta$ for some $\theta>0$. The constants are uniform in mesh, common pins, and auxiliary finite real fields. Either law can be replaced by a mixture; the comparison also holds after a common conditional randomization, across fixed polygonal collars of positive relative width, and for different continuations whose reference interactions agree in the separating chart. Free or fixed physical boundaries can intersect such collars.* *In particular, let $W>0$ and $f$ be bounded functions supported in $B_w(z)$, let $s/w>1$ leave a fixed relative collar, and put $$\label{ref:normalized-weights} Z=\langle W\rangle_0,\qquad N=Z^{-1}P_{s,z}W,\qquad M=Z^{-1}P_{s,z}(fW).$$ Then $$\label{ref:weight-bounds} C^{-1}\le N\le C,\qquad \left\lVert M\right\rVert_\infty\le C\left\lVert f\right\rVert_\infty, \qquad \langle N\rangle_0=1,\quad \langle M\rangle_0=\frac{\langle fW\rangle_0}{Z}.$$ The bounds are independent of the amplitude of $W$. If $W$ and $f$ are spin-flip even, then $N$ and $M$ are even.
  - Explanation: Across a fixed relative buffer in the critical reference model, conditional laws of inner spins under different outer data are comparable by a constant, which gives bounded normalised positive weights independent of the weight amplitude.
- **claim-finite-dictionary** (bound, [source](#stmt-ref-dictionary)): Fix concentric inner and outer squares with a sufficiently large fixed ratio of radii and fixed relative buffers. Rescale the inner radius to one; the resulting mesh is $1/w$ when the original radius is $w$ in lattice units. For every $\eta>0$ there are an integer $N$ and a fine-mesh threshold such that, at each finer mesh, one can choose outer cutting configurations $\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_N$ with $$\label{ref:finite-tests} \left\lVert PF\right\rVert_\infty \le \max_{1\le k\le N}\|PF(\tau_k)\|+\eta\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty$$ for every bounded function $F$ supported in the inner square. Here $P$ averages to the outer contour. The number $N$ is independent of the mesh and of $F$; the actual configurations may depend on the mesh. Consequently, for a random inner function $F$ and $q\ge1$, $$\label{ref:finite-moment-tests} \bigl(\mathbb E\left\lVert PF\right\rVert_\infty^q\bigr)^{1/q} \le N^{1/q}\max_k\bigl(\mathbb E\|PF(\tau_k)\|^q\bigr)^{1/q} +\eta\bigl(\mathbb E\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty^q\bigr)^{1/q}.$$
  - Explanation: An averaged sup norm over all outer configurations is controlled by finitely many tested configurations up to an arbitrarily small multiple of the input sup norm, with a count independent of the mesh.
- **claim-block-carriers** (bound, [source](#stmt-block-carrier)): The construction has exact finite dependence carriers. Its principal outputs fit in a fixed halo $S$ at scale $R$. An animal output generated by input occurrences of sizes $n_1,\ldots,n_k$ has size $$n'\le\sum_{a=1}^k\left(C+\frac{Cn_a}{L}\right), \label{block:compression}$$ where $C$ depends only on the fixed protective neighborhoods and not on $L$. Both the spin carrier and the dependence carrier satisfy these statements. The same bound holds for the footprint of a saved scalar.
  - Explanation: One blocking step has finite dependence carriers: principal outputs fit in a fixed halo and animal outputs have size controlled linearly by the input sizes with a gain from the scale ratio.
- **claim-exact-partition-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-block-exact)): For sufficiently small $\delta$ at fixed $L$, let a finite-volume real background have finitely many possible anchors, satisfy <a href="#block:tail-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="block:tail-hypothesis">[block:tail-hypothesis]</a>, and have finite principal norms. Every admissible source jet with finitely many labels then has a transformed centered jet $H'(\zeta)$ and a saved scalar jet $s'(\zeta)$ such that $$\int e^{H(\zeta)}\,\,\mathrm d\mu_0 =e^{s'(\zeta)}\int e^{H'(\zeta)}\,\,\mathrm d\mu_0. \label{block:partition-identity}$$ The equality is coefficientwise in the square-free variables and holds for every disorder realization and every choice of decision seeds. Consequently the normalized source partition function is $$\frac{\int e^{H(\zeta)}\,\,\mathrm d\mu_0}{\int e^{H(0)}\,\,\mathrm d\mu_0} =e^{s'(\zeta)-s'(0)} \frac{\int e^{H'(\zeta)}\,\,\mathrm d\mu_0}{\int e^{H'(0)}\,\,\mathrm d\mu_0}. \label{block:normalized-identity}$$ All pure bad output is principal, and the linearization at zero is <a href="#block:linear" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="block:linear">[block:linear]</a>.
  - Explanation: For every disorder realisation and decision seed, the original source partition integral equals a saved scalar jet times the integral of the transformed centred jet, and the normalised version follows by division.
- **claim-tree-coefficients** (bound, [source](#stmt-block-tree)): Fix an arbitrary real bad background and its selected squares. For $k$ small or marked occurrences with contact graph $G$, their connected coefficient before centering satisfies $$c(I)=0\quad\text{if }G\text{ is disconnected},\qquad \\|c(I)\\|_\infty\le 2^{k-1}\tau(G)\prod_{a=1}^k\\|f_a\\|_\infty \quad\text{otherwise}, \label{block:tree-coefficient}$$ where $\tau(G)$ is the number of spanning trees and $\tau(G)=1$ for a single vertex. For any of the fixed output weights, sufficiently large $L$ allows summation of the ordered order-$k$ coefficients, with their $1/k!$, at a fixed output anchor at cost $C_L^k$ times the product of the input weighted bounds. This holds also with a fixed larger output weight, before or after centering, and for a fixed finite number of marked occurrences or substitutions of input norm factors.* *In particular the bounds can be realized by deterministic nonnegative coefficient majorants that retain every separate factor $\\|f_a\\|_\infty$. If all but one of these factors are small backgrounds, summing terms of order at least two leaves a factor at most $C_L\delta$ times the retained factor’s weighted input bound. This last statement also holds with a marked retained factor under the normalized bad operators. The analogous multilinear maps on indexed projective tensors have the same bounds, after the indicated allocation of weights.
  - Explanation: Connected coefficients vanish for disconnected contact graphs and are bounded by the number of spanning trees times the product of input norms; weighted sums over placements converge with a cost polynomial in the block ratio.
- **claim-large-footprint-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-block-diameter)): Retain the animal footprint of a connected coefficient before projecting onto constants. For background coefficients from the small-entry expansion whose footprint has physical diameter at least $D$, the total absolute sum at a fixed output anchor is at most $$C_L\delta e^{-cD/R}.$$ The same gain can be retained together with any fixed number of specified input norm factors, including marked factors. On a torus of side $N$, writing $m=N/R$, the total absolute sum of small-expansion scalar terms whose footprints wrap or fail to fit in a chart of fixed relative size is at most $C_L\delta m^2e^{-cm}$. Here $m$ is sufficiently large relative to the fixed halos. Pure bad scalar terms have chart footprints and do not contribute to this last sum.
  - Explanation: Connected coefficients with large footprints, and torus scalar terms that wrap or fail to fit in a chart, carry an exponentially small total weight.
- **claim-tail-preservation** (bound, [source](#stmt-block-tails)): For sufficiently large $L$ and then sufficiently small $\delta$, the transformed nonprincipal background satisfies $\\|h'_{A,c}\\|_\infty\le\delta e^{-w\|A\|}$ for every disorder realization. More precisely, with the same output weight $w$, $$\sup_{(A,c)}e^{w\|A\|}\\|h'_{A,c}\\|_\infty \le C_L\delta^2+C_Le^{-cL}\delta. \label{block:tail-propagation}$$
  - Explanation: After one step the nonprincipal background keeps the same exponential tail bound, for every disorder realisation, once the block ratio is large and the threshold small.
- **claim-finite-volume-implementation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-block-finite)): The preceding transformation and partition identity apply to finite boxes with fixed or free boundary data, and to tori whose side is an integer multiple of the working scales, while the scale is sufficiently below the side length. The input finite-volume lists have finitely many possible anchors and admissible source jets. On a torus, wrapping interactions remain in torus animal slots. At every fixed number of steps the principal norms have deterministic bounds uniform in volume, disorder and decision seeds whenever the microscopic principal norms have such a bound. The total list sums are finite, with bounds that may grow with the volume. Bulk slots whose full ancestral footprints avoid the boundary agree exactly with the plane construction. Infinite-plane lists require only the indexed and rooted sums proved above; no infinite-volume partition integral is used in their definition.
  - Explanation: The transformation and its exact identity hold in finite boxes and tori, with volume-uniform principal bounds at fixed step number and exact agreement with the plane in the bulk.
- **claim-taylor-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-norm-taylor)): Under <a href="#norm:input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="norm:input">[norm:input]</a>, the mean and the raw moment and cumulant arrays with at most four arguments are given by the ordinary zero-background Taylor rule, retaining total background degree at most four, with error $O(s^5)$ in the prescribed weighted indexed projective norms. The coefficient maps are bounded uniformly in the scale. An output cumulant vanishes if its dependence carriers split into two nonempty mutually separated families.
  - Explanation: Mean, raw moments and cumulants with at most four arguments follow the zero-background Taylor rule up to a fifth-order error in the weighted projective norms, and cumulants vanish across separated carriers.
- **claim-tilt-attenuation** (bound, [source](#stmt-norm-attenuation)): Let $B$ and $F$ be even functions supported in a square of radius $w$, with a proportional empty collar, and let $P_R$ average to a surrounding square of radius $R\ge Cw$. There is a constant $C$, independent of the real function $B$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \inf_{a\in\mathbb R}\left\lVert\frac{P_R(e^BF)}{P_R e^B}-a\right\rVert_\infty &\le C\frac wR\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty,\label{norm:tilted-function}\\ \inf_{a\in\mathbb R}\left\lVert\log P_R e^B-a\right\rVert_\infty &\le C\frac wR\min\{1,\left\lVert B\right\rVert_\infty\}. \label{norm:tilted-log} \end{aligned}$$ The centered versions satisfy the same bounds after multiplying $C$ by at most two. In particular, $B$ need not define a ferromagnetic perturbation or satisfy a smallness assumption.
  - Explanation: For even tilt and observable supported in a smaller square, the normalised tilted conditional expectation and the logarithm of the tilt are constant up to an error proportional to the radius ratio, with no smallness assumption on the tilt.
- **claim-moment-reproduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-norm-reproduction)): Choose $L$ sufficiently large and then $\delta$ sufficiently small. Under <a href="#norm:input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="norm:input">[norm:input]</a>, $$\begin{aligned} \left\lVert h'\right\rVert_{v,p}&\le Bs/8+C_L^*B_2s+o(s),\label{norm:high-reproduction}\\ \left\lVert h'\right\rVert_{v,2}&\le C_{\eta,L}F_*s+\varepsilon_* Bs+o(s). \label{norm:L2-reproduction} \end{aligned}$$ Here $C_L^*$ grows at most polynomially in $L$. For the second inequality, assume that each output principal slot has diagonal second-moment bound $$\label{norm:diagonal} \sup_\sigma\mathbb E\|h'_a(\sigma)\|^2\le e^{-2v}F_*^2s^2, \qquad F_*\ge1.$$ A bound on its raw second tensor implies this hypothesis. One can choose $\varepsilon_*<[100(1+C_L^*)]^{-1}$: first use the long-slot suppression, then choose the dictionary accuracy $\eta$ and the mesh threshold $r_0$. These choices do not depend on $B,B_2,F_*$.
  - Explanation: High moment norms reproduce with a small leading coefficient, and the second moment norm is recovered through the finite dictionary and a diagonal second-moment hypothesis.
- **claim-source-taylor-mixed** (bound, [source](#stmt-norm-source)): Under the preceding assumptions, moments with $q$ source occurrences and at most two background arguments obey the ordinary Taylor rule through total background degree two, with error $O(U^qs^3)$ in the weighted projective norms. Background arguments already present in a tensor count toward that degree. With fewer source arguments the corresponding bound follows by Hölder. Moreover, $$\label{norm:source-difference} \left\lVert u'-L^dTu\right\rVert_{v,q}=O(Us),$$ and the output satisfies $$\label{norm:mixed-output} \sup_{a,b}e^{vq n_a/p+vn_b} (\mathbb E\left\lVert u'_a\right\rVert_\infty^q\left\lVert h'_b\right\rVert_\infty^p)^{1/p} \le U^{q/p}\{B_s s/8+C_{L,B}s+o(s)\}.$$ The choices of $p,L,\delta$ and the weights can be made before $B_s$ and $U$. All these estimates retain any saved scalar’s carrier until after its weighted estimate is taken.
  - Explanation: Moments involving source occurrences obey the Taylor rule through background degree two, the source map tracks scaled linear transport, and mixed source-background moments reproduce with a small coefficient.
- **claim-background-linear-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lin-background)): Fix the finite set of weights needed in Section <a href="#sec:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norms">4</a>. By taking $L$ sufficiently large, all the following contraction constants can be made smaller than any prescribed $\kappa>0$: $$\\|T_jm\\|\le\kappa\\|m\\|\quad(x_{r_j}(m)=0),\qquad \\|T_j^{(2)}K\\|\le\kappa\\|K\\|\quad(g_{r_j}(K)=0),$$ and $\\|T_j^{(k)}A\\|\le\kappa\\|A\\|$ for homogeneous connected arrays of orders $k=3,4$. Norms here and below have the fixed input and output weights allocated to the corresponding estimate. The bounds are uniform in $j$. The two projections above intertwine transport with multipliers $L$ and $1$.
  - Explanation: Linear background transport contracts strongly off the mean and variance-density directions, while the two projections intertwine transport with fixed multipliers.
- **claim-marked-linear-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lin-marked)): For each fixed finite set of positive integers $q$, the operators $\mathcal T_j^{(q)}$ are bounded uniformly in $j,y,L$. On $\ker Q_{j,q,y}$ their norms tend to zero as $L\to\infty$. A connected array comprising this marked tensor as one grouped argument and at least one even background argument contracts without removal of a line. The one-source kernel estimate also holds pathwise, and hence in each of the weighted random-list $L^p$ norms used in Section <a href="#sec:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norms">4</a>.
  - Explanation: Scaled odd transport on marked tensors is uniformly bounded and contracts on the kernel of the normalised spin charge, and contracts with an even background argument present.
- **claim-formal-profiles** (bound, [source](#stmt-coef-profiles)): For $j\ge1$, the arrays $m_j^*,W_j^*,Y_j^*,Z_{j,q,y}^*$ have bounded indexed projective norms, uniformly in $j$ and $y$ and for every fixed finite set of $q$’s. Moreover $K_j^*=V_j$ and the degree-zero marked list is $w_{j,y}$. The coefficients $b_j$ and $c_{j,q}(y)$ are uniformly bounded.
  - Explanation: The formal mean, third and fourth cumulant arrays and the marked cross-cumulant array are bounded uniformly in scale and mark position, and the covariance profile equals the exact representative.
- **claim-finite-insertion-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-invariance)): Each coefficient of $\mathcal E_j$ and $\mathcal S_j$ in the formal variables is independent of $j$. It equals the corresponding normalized charge of the original microscopic product and, for $\mathcal S_j$, its spin insertion.
  - Explanation: Normalised energy and spin charges of finitely many insertions do not change under successive blocking steps and equal those of the original microscopic product.
- **claim-marginal-coefficient-averages** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-averages)): The coefficients in <a href="#coef:b" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="coef:b">[coef:b]</a>–<a href="#coef:c" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="coef:c">[coef:c]</a> satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \sum_{j<J}b_j&=-4\pi a^2\log r_J+o(\log r_J),\label{coef:bsum}\\ \sum_{j<J}c_{j,q}(y)&=\binom q2\frac{\pi a^2}{2}\log r_J +o(\log r_J),\label{coef:csum} \end{aligned}$$ where the second error is uniform in $y$ for each fixed $q$. Equivalently, their Cesàro means are $-4\pi a^2\log L$ and $\binom q2(\pi a^2/2)\log L$.
  - Explanation: Summed over scales, the covariance increments and marked charge increments grow logarithmically with explicit coefficients determined by the energy and energy-spin products.
- **claim-bounded-first-charge** (bound, [source](#stmt-coef-bounded-first)): There is a constant independent of $J$ and $y$ such that $$\left\|\sum_{j<J}c_{j,1}(y)\right\|\le C.$$
  - Explanation: For a single source the summed charge increments stay bounded uniformly, because the relevant quadratic invariant coefficient vanishes exactly.
- **claim-finite-prefix-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-flow-prefix)): There are fixed constants $X,C$ and $\rho<1$ such that, for sufficiently small $D>0$, every trajectory kept through scale $N$ satisfies the background tail and moment bounds of Section <a href="#sec:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norms">4</a> with $s=s_j$, and $$\label{flow:rough} \left\lVert m_j\right\rVert+\left\lVert K_j\right\rVert\le CG_j,\qquad \left\lVert W_j\right\rVert+\left\lVert Y_j\right\rVert\le CG_j^2,\qquad \tfrac12G_j<g_j<2G_j .$$ The tensor and moment bounds also hold for the tested next step, with a larger fixed constant for its mean. Moreover, for $1\le j\le N$, $$\begin{aligned} K_j-g_jV_j&=O\bigl(G_j(G_j+\rho^j)\bigr),\label{flow:cov-profile}\\ m_j-g_jm_j^*&=O\bigl(G_j(G_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})\bigr), \label{flow:mean-profile}\\ W_j-g_j^2W_j^*,\quad Y_j-g_j^2Y_j^* &=O\bigl(G_j^2(s_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})\bigr). \label{flow:higher-profile} \end{aligned}$$ Here $V_j,m_j^*,W_j^*,Y_j^*$ are the bounded formal profiles of Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">6</a>. For $0\le j\le N$, including the tested next covariance, $$\label{flow:variance-recursion} g_{j+1}=g_j+b_jg_j^2+ O\bigl(G_j^2(s_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})\bigr).$$ The constants are independent of $N$ and of the kept trajectory.
  - Explanation: On any kept prefix of scales the background cumulants obey the stated norm bounds and profile comparisons, and the variance coordinate satisfies a quadratic recursion with controlled errors.
- **claim-tuned-trajectory** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flow-tuned)): For every sufficiently small $D>0$ there is $t_*(D)=O(D^2)$ whose background trajectory satisfies $\|x_j\|\le XG_j$ at every scale. All the bounds of Proposition <a href="#flow:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flow:prefix">23</a> hold along this trajectory. In the profile estimates its terminal geometric term may be omitted.
  - Explanation: For small disorder there is a tuned temperature parameter for which the background trajectory stays inside the thermal tube at every scale.
- **claim-variance-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-flow-variance)): Along every tuned trajectory obtained above, $$\label{flow:variance-asymptotic} g_j\sim\frac1{B_0j}\quad(j\to\infty),\qquad \sum_{j\ge0}\|g_{j+1}-g_j\|\le C\bar g.$$ The uniform comparison <a href="#flow:uniform-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:uniform-ratio">[flow:uniform-ratio]</a> also holds over all $j\ge0$ as $D\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Along a tuned trajectory the variance coordinate decays inversely to the scale index with a total variation bounded by the initial size.
- **claim-marked-kernel-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-mark-kernel)): For each $q\in\mathcal Q$ and sufficiently small $D>0$, $A_{j,q}>0$ and there are fixed constants $C_*,B_s,C$ such that, at every scale, $$\begin{aligned} \left\lVert u_j\right\rVert_{v,q}&\le C_*A_{j,q}^{1/q},\label{mark:lq}\\ \bigl(\mathbb E\left\lVert u_{j,i}\right\rVert^q\left\lVert h_{j,m}\right\rVert^p\bigr)^{1/p} &\le (C_*A_{j,q}^{1/q})^{q/p}B_s s_j e^{-vq n_i/p-vn_m}.\label{mark:mixed} \end{aligned}$$ For $j\ge1$ one has the stronger estimate $$\label{mark:kernel-small} \left\lVert u_j-\alpha_jw_{j,y}\right\rVert_{v,q} \le C A_{j,q}^{1/q}s_j.$$ The notation $\alpha_jw_{j,y}$ places the representative in its marked principal slot. The allowed disorder can be chosen uniformly for the fixed finite collection $\mathcal Q$.
  - Explanation: The marked list has weighted moment norms controlled by the charge moment, mixed source-background moments are bounded, and the part transverse to the spin charge is small.
- **claim-marked-connected-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mark-connected-comparison)): For $j\ge1$, with the bounded formal profile $Z_{j,q,y}^*$ from Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">6</a>, $$\begin{aligned} C_{2,j}&=O(A_js_j^3),\label{mark:c2}\\ C_{1,j}&=A_jg_j Z_{j,q,y}^* +O\bigl(A_jG_j(s_j+\rho^j)\bigr),\label{mark:c1}\\ S_j&=A_jw_{j,y}^{\otimes q}+O(A_js_j).\label{mark:s-profile} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently the marked charge moments obey $$\label{mark:scalar-recursion} A_{j+1}=A_j\left(1+c_{j,q}(y)g_j+ O\bigl(G_j(s_j+\rho^j)\bigr)\right).$$ The recurrence also holds at $j=0$, where its error is allowed to be $O(G_0)$.
  - Explanation: Connected cumulants of the marked tensor with background fields are compared with the formal profile, giving a multiplicative recurrence for the charge moments.
- **claim-marked-moment-asymptotics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mark-moments)): For each $q\in\mathcal Q$ and fixed sufficiently small $D>0$, $$\label{mark:asymptotic} A_{j,q}=j^{\binom q2/8+o(1)}\qquad(j\to\infty),$$ uniformly in the marked site $y$. In addition there is a constant independent of sufficiently small $D$ such that $$\label{mark:second-uniform} A_{j,2}\le C(1+B_0j\bar g)^{1/4}\qquad(j\ge0).$$
  - Explanation: Charge moments grow as a power of the scale index with exponent given by the binomial coefficient over eight, and the second moment has a uniform bound in terms of the comparison sequence.
- **claim-first-moment-bounded** (bound, [source](#stmt-mark-first)): Uniformly in $j$ and $y$, for sufficiently small $D>0$, $$\label{mark:first-bound} \frac1C\le A_{j,1}=\mathbb E\alpha_j\le C.$$ More precisely, $\sup_{j,y}\|A_{j,1}-1\|\to0$ as $D\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: The first charge moment stays bounded above and away from zero, and tends to one uniformly as the disorder parameter vanishes.
- **claim-joint-source-merge** (bound, [source](#stmt-read-merge)): At the stopping scale <a href="#read:stop" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:stop">[read:stop]</a>, the sum of the sup norms of the joint-source list and the absolute values of its saved scalar increments, including their subsequent scaling, has $L^2$ norm at most $$C s_J J^{3/8+o(1)}=J^{-1/8+o(1)}. \label{read:merge-bound}$$ Replacing $U_{J,1}U_{J,2}$ by $Z_Jw_{J,y_1}w_{J,y_2}$ has the same $L^2$ bound on its sup norm.
  - Explanation: At the stopping scale, joint-source terms and their saved scalar increments are small in square-integrable norm, and replacing the product of singleton lists by the product of principal amplitudes costs the same.
- **claim-disorder-moments-at-tuning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-read-moments)): Let $C_{*,\omega}(y_1,y_2)$ be the free infinite-volume spin correlation at $t_*(D)$, and let $J$ be determined by <a href="#read:stop" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:stop">[read:stop]</a>. For every fixed sufficiently small $D>0$, uniformly in the two marks as their separation tends to infinity, $$r_J^{2d}\mathbb EC_{*,\omega}(y_1,y_2)\asymp1, \qquad r_J^{4d}\mathbb EC_{*,\omega}(y_1,y_2)^2 =J^{1/4+o(1)}. \label{read:moment-conclusion}$$
  - Explanation: At the tuned parameter, the scale-normalised first moment of the free correlation is of order one and the second moment grows like a power of the scale index with exponent one quarter.
- **claim-wired-arm-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-read-arm)): For any square box of radius $n$ centered at a lattice site $y$, let $\phi^w_{n,*,\omega}$ be the FK–Ising law at $t_*(D)$ with wired boundary. Then $$\mathbb E\left[\phi^w_{n,*,\omega} (y\longleftrightarrow\partial\Lambda_n(y))^{400}\right] \le n^{-400/8+o(1)}. \label{read:arm-bound}$$ The estimate is uniform in $y$. It also holds if the fixed wall is one lattice layer inside or outside the indicated square, including the convention with bonds crossing to an exterior wired layer.
  - Explanation: The high moment of the wired arm probability in a box decays as a negative power of the radius, uniformly in the centre and for the stated wall conventions.
- **claim-torus-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-crit-torus-duality)): Under the intersection pairing, $$\label{crit:homology-annihilator} H(A^*)=H(A)^\perp,\qquad r(A^*)=2-r(A).$$ If $v_{e^*}^*=2/v_e$, then complement duality carries the torus FK law at $v$ to a measure whose density with respect to the torus FK law at $v^*$ lies between $1/4$ and $4$.
  - Explanation: On the torus the homology of the complementary dual configuration is the annihilator of that of the primal one, and complement duality maps the random-cluster law to one comparable to the dual law by a bounded density.
- **claim-torus-nondegeneracy-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-crit-torus-transfer)): Fix a sufficiently large integer $Q$. After choosing the smallness constants of the construction sufficiently small, there is $a_1>0$ such that, for $\ell_J=Qr_J$ and every nonzero $\chi$, $$\label{crit:tuned-homology} a_1\le \mathbb E\phi_{\mathbb T_{\ell_J},t_*,\omega}(\mathcal H_\chi) \le1-a_1$$ for all sufficiently large $J$.
  - Explanation: Crossing nondegeneracy of the reference torus transfers to the disordered torus at the tuned parameter for large scales.
- **claim-torus-threshold-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-crit-threshold)): Fix a compact parameter window containing $0$ and $t_*(D)$, with uniformly positive, bounded couplings. If $\mathcal A$ is any increasing translation-invariant event on $\mathbb T_\ell$ and $f_\ell(t)=\mathbb E\phi_{\mathbb T_\ell,t,\omega}(\mathcal A)$, then $$\label{crit:threshold-inequality} f_\ell'(t)\ge c\log\ell\, f_\ell(t)(1-f_\ell(t)), \qquad \ell\ge3,$$ with $c>0$ independent of $\ell$ and $\mathcal A$.
  - Explanation: For any increasing translation-invariant torus event, the environment-averaged probability has a derivative bounded below by a logarithmic factor times the product of the probability and its complement.
- **claim-tuning-is-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-crit-tuning-zero)): For every sufficiently small fixed $D>0$, the tuned parameter is $t_*(D)=0$.
  - Explanation: Self-duality at zero combined with the threshold inequality and nondegeneracy at the tuned value forces the tuned parameter to be zero.
- **claim-subcritical-arm-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-crit-subcritical-arm)): For every fixed $t'<0$ in the parameter window and sufficiently small fixed $D>0$, $$\label{crit:subcritical-decay} \mathbb E\phi^{\mathrm w}_{t',\omega} (0\longleftrightarrow\partial\Lambda_n) \le C_{t',D}n^{-6}.$$
  - Explanation: Below zero, the averaged wired arm probability decays polynomially, obtained from a randomised shell exploration and the monotone decision-tree inequality on a good-environment event.
- **claim-magnetization-transition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-crit-identification)): For every sufficiently small fixed $D>0$, the disorder-averaged plus magnetization vanishes at $t\le0$ and is positive at every $t>0$.
  - Explanation: The averaged plus magnetization vanishes at nonpositive parameter and is positive for every positive parameter, so zero is the magnetization threshold.

        "exact_quote": "Consider a finite critical reference graph in a square chart. Let $I,J$ and $V_{\\mathrm{pin}}$ be pairwise disjoint vertex sets, and prescribe common spin values on $V_{\\mathrm{pin}}$. Suppose $I\\subset B_w(z)$ and $J\\subset B_R(z)^c$. For $R\\ge(1+c)w$ with fixed $c>0$, every assignment $a$ on $I$ and assignments $\\tau,\\tau'$ on $J$ satisfy $$\\label{ref:density-ratio}\n C_c^{-1}\\le\n \\frac{\\mathbb P_0(\\sigma_I=a\\mid\\sigma_J=\\tau,\\sigma_{V_{\\mathrm{pin}}})}\n      {\\mathbb P_0(\\sigma_I=a\\mid\\sigma_J=\\tau',\\sigma_{V_{\\mathrm{pin}}})}\n \\le C_c.$$ For $R\\ge8w\\ge8$, the absolute logarithm of this ratio is at most $C(w/R)^\\theta$ for some $\\theta>0$. The constants are uniform in mesh, common pins, and auxiliary finite real fields. Free or fixed physical boundaries can intersect such collars.*\n\n\n*In particular, let $W>0$ and $f$ be bounded functions supported in $B_w(z)$, let $s/w>1$ leave a fixed relative collar, and put $$\\label{ref:normalized-weights}\n Z=\\langle W\\rangle_0,\\qquad\n N=Z^{-1}P_{s,z}W,\\qquad M=Z^{-1}P_{s,z}(fW).$$ Then $$\\label{ref:weight-bounds}\n C^{-1}\\le N\\le C,\\qquad \\left\\lVert M\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le C\\left\\lVert f\\right\\rVert_\\infty,\n \\qquad \\langle N\\rangle_0=1,\\quad\n \\langle M\\rangle_0=\\frac{\\langle fW\\rangle_0}{Z}.$$ The bounds are independent of the amplitude of $W$.         "exact_quote": "Fix concentric inner and outer squares with a sufficiently large fixed ratio of radii and fixed relative buffers.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-block-exact",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently small $\\delta$ at fixed $L$, let a finite-volume real background have finitely many possible anchors, satisfy <a href=\"#block:tail-hypothesis\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"block:tail-hypothesis\">[block:tail-hypothesis]</a>, and have finite principal norms. Every admissible source jet with finitely many labels then has a transformed centered jet $H'(\\zeta)$ and a saved scalar jet $s'(\\zeta)$ such that $$\\int e^{H(\\zeta)}\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_0\n       =e^{s'(\\zeta)}\\int e^{H'(\\zeta)}\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_0.\n \\label{block:partition-identity}$$ The equality is coefficientwise in the square-free variables and holds for every disorder realization and every choice of decision seeds. Consequently the normalized source partition function is $$\\frac{\\int e^{H(\\zeta)}\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_0}{\\int e^{H(0)}\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_0}\n =e^{s'(\\zeta)-s'(0)}\n   \\frac{\\int e^{H'(\\zeta)}\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_0}{\\int e^{H'(0)}\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_0}.\n \\label{block:normalized-identity}$$ All pure bad output is principal, and the linearization at zero is <a href=\"#block:linear\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"block:linear\">[block:linear]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.3"
      },
      "evidence": [],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-block-tree",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an arbitrary real bad background and its selected squares. For $k$ small or marked occurrences with contact graph $G$, their connected coefficient before centering satisfies $$c(I)=0\\quad\\text{if }G\\text{ is disconnected},\\qquad\n \\|c(I)\\|_\\infty\\le\n       2^{k-1}\\tau(G)\\prod_{a=1}^k\\|f_a\\|_\\infty\n       \\quad\\text{otherwise},\n \\label{block:tree-coefficient}$$ where $\\tau(G)$ is the number of spanning trees and $\\tau(G)=1$ for a single vertex. For any of the fixed output weights, sufficiently large $L$ allows summation of the ordered order-$k$ coefficients, with their $1/k!$, at a fixed output anchor at cost $C_L^k$ times the product of the input weighted bounds. This holds also with a fixed larger output weight, before or after centering, and for a fixed finite number of marked occurrences or substitutions of input norm factors.*\n\n*In particular the bounds can be realized by deterministic nonnegative coefficient majorants that retain every separate factor $\\|f_a\\|_\\infty$. If all but one of these factors are small backgrounds, summing terms of order at least two leaves a factor at most $C_L\\delta$ times the retained factor’s weighted input bound. This last statement also holds with a marked retained factor under the normalized bad operators. The analogous multilinear maps on indexed projective tensors have the same bounds, after the indicated allocation of weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.3"
      },
      "evidence": [],

For any of the fixed output weights, sufficiently large $L$ allows summation of the ordered order-$k$ coefficients, with their $1/k!$, at a fixed output anchor at cost $C_L^k$ times the product of the input weighted bounds. This holds also with a fixed larger output weight, before or after centering, and for a fixed finite number of marked occurrences or substitutions of input norm factors.*\n\n*In particular the bounds can be realized by deterministic nonnegative coefficient majorants that retain every separate factor $\\|f_a\\|_\\infty$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-block-finite",
        "exact_quote": "The preceding transformation and partition identity apply to finite boxes with fixed or free boundary data, and to tori whose side is an integer multiple of the working scales, while the scale is sufficiently below the side length. The input finite-volume lists have finitely many possible anchors and admissible source jets. On a torus, wrapping interactions remain in torus animal slots. At every fixed number of steps the principal norms have deterministic bounds uniform in volume, disorder and decision seeds whenever the microscopic principal norms have such a bound. The total list sums are finite, with bounds that may grow with the volume. Bulk slots whose full ancestral footprints avoid the boundary agree exactly with the plane construction. Infinite-plane lists require only the indexed and rooted sums proved above; no infinite-volume partition integral is used in their definition.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.3"
      },
      "evidence": [],

        "anchor": "stmt-norm-attenuation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ and $F$ be even functions supported in a square of radius $w$, with a proportional empty collar, and let $P_R$ average to a surrounding square of radius $R\\ge Cw$. There is a constant $C$, independent of the real function $B$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\inf_{a\\in\\mathbb R}\\left\\lVert\\frac{P_R(e^BF)}{P_R e^B}-a\\right\\rVert_\\infty\n       &\\le C\\frac wR\\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_\\infty,\\label{norm:tilted-function}\\\\\n \\inf_{a\\in\\mathbb R}\\left\\lVert\\log P_R e^B-a\\right\\rVert_\\infty\n       &\\le C\\frac wR\\min\\{1,\\left\\lVert B\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\}.\n                                      \\label{norm:tilted-log}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The centered versions satisfy the same bounds after multiplying $C$ by at most two. In particular, $B$ need not define a ferromagnetic perturbation or satisfy a smallness assumption.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.3"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "Let $B$ and $F$ be even functions supported in a square of radius $w$, with a proportional empty collar, and let $P_R$ average to a surrounding square of radius $R\\ge Cw$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lin-marked",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed finite set of positive integers $q$, the operators $\\mathcal T_j^{(q)}$ are bounded uniformly in $j,y,L$. On $\\ker Q_{j,q,y}$ their norms tend to zero as $L\\to\\infty$. A connected array comprising this marked tensor as one grouped argument and at least one even background argument contracts without removal of a line.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flow-prefix",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed constants $X,C$ and $\\rho<1$ such that, for sufficiently small $D>0$, every trajectory kept through scale $N$ satisfies the background tail and moment bounds of Section <a href=\"#sec:norms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:norms\">4</a> with $s=s_j$, and $$\\label{flow:rough}\n \\left\\lVert m_j\\right\\rVert+\\left\\lVert K_j\\right\\rVert\\le CG_j,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert W_j\\right\\rVert+\\left\\lVert Y_j\\right\\rVert\\le CG_j^2,\\qquad\n \\tfrac12G_j<g_j<2G_j .$$ The tensor and moment bounds also hold for the tested next step, with a larger fixed constant for its mean. Moreover, for $1\\le j\\le N$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n K_j-g_jV_j&=O\\bigl(G_j(G_j+\\rho^j)\\bigr),\\label{flow:cov-profile}\\\\\n m_j-g_jm_j^*&=O\\bigl(G_j(G_j+\\rho^j+\\rho^{N-j})\\bigr),\n \\label{flow:mean-profile}\\\\\n W_j-g_j^2W_j^*,\\quad Y_j-g_j^2Y_j^*\n &=O\\bigl(G_j^2(s_j+\\rho^j+\\rho^{N-j})\\bigr).\n \\label{flow:higher-profile}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $V_j,m_j^*,W_j^*,Y_j^*$ are the bounded formal profiles of Section <a href=\"#sec:coefficients\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:coefficients\">6</a>. For $0\\le j\\le N$, including the tested next covariance, $$\\label{flow:variance-recursion}\n g_{j+1}=g_j+b_jg_j^2+\n O\\bigl(G_j^2(s_j+\\rho^j+\\rho^{N-j})\\bigr).$$ The constants are independent of $N$ and of the kept trajectory.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.3"
      },
      "evidence": [],
      "exact_statement": "There are fixed constants $X,C$ and $\\rho<1$ such that, for sufficiently small $D>0$, every trajectory kept through scale $N$ satisfies the background tail and moment bounds of Section <a href=\"#sec:norms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:norms\">4</a> with $s=s_j$, and $$\\label{flow:rough}\n \\left\\lVert m_j\\right\\rVert+\\left\\lVert K_j\\right\\rVert\\le CG_j,\\qquad\n \\left\\lVert W_j\\right\\rVert+\\left\\lVert Y_j\\right\\rVert\\le CG_j^2,\\qquad\n \\tfrac12G_j<g_j<2G_j .$$ The tensor and moment bounds also hold for the tested next step, with a larger fixed constant for its mean.         "exact_quote": "For each $q\\in\\mathcal Q$ and sufficiently small $D>0$, $A_{j,q}>0$ and there are fixed constants $C_*,B_s,C$ such that, at every scale, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\left\\lVert u_j\\right\\rVert_{v,q}&\\le C_*A_{j,q}^{1/q},\\label{mark:lq}\\\\\n \\bigl(\\mathbb E\\left\\lVert u_{j,i}\\right\\rVert^q\\left\\lVert h_{j,m}\\right\\rVert^p\\bigr)^{1/p}\n &\\le (C_*A_{j,q}^{1/q})^{q/p}B_s s_j\n       e^{-vq n_i/p-vn_m}.\\label{mark:mixed}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $j\\ge1$ one has the stronger estimate $$\\label{mark:kernel-small}\n \\left\\lVert u_j-\\alpha_jw_{j,y}\\right\\rVert_{v,q}\n       \\le C A_{j,q}^{1/q}s_j.$$ The notation $\\alpha_jw_{j,y}$ places the representative in its marked principal slot.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mark-connected-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For $j\\ge1$, with the bounded formal profile $Z_{j,q,y}^*$ from Section <a href=\"#sec:coefficients\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:coefficients\">6</a>, $$\\begin{aligned}\n C_{2,j}&=O(A_js_j^3),\\label{mark:c2}\\\\\n C_{1,j}&=A_jg_j Z_{j,q,y}^*\n       +O\\bigl(A_jG_j(s_j+\\rho^j)\\bigr),\\label{mark:c1}\\\\\n S_j&=A_jw_{j,y}^{\\otimes q}+O(A_js_j).\\label{mark:s-profile}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the marked charge moments obey $$\\label{mark:scalar-recursion}\n A_{j+1}=A_j\\left(1+c_{j,q}(y)g_j+\n                    O\\bigl(G_j(s_j+\\rho^j)\\bigr)\\right).$$ The recurrence also holds at $j=0$, where its error is allowed to be $O(G_0)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **critical inverse temperature and averaged magnetization** ([source](#eq:critical-definition)): The physical critical inverse temperature is $$\label{eq:critical-definition} m_\varepsilon(\beta)=\mathbb E\!\left[\lim_{n\to\infty} \langle\sigma_0\rangle^+_{n,\beta,\omega}\right], \qquad \beta_c(\varepsilon)=\inf\{\beta\ge0:m_\varepsilon(\beta)>0\}.$$ Ferromagnetic monotonicity gives the plus limit. Comparison with constant bonds, with $\beta_0=\tfrac12\log(1+\sqrt2)$, gives $$0<\frac{\beta_0}{1+\varepsilon}\le\beta_c(\varepsilon) \le\frac{\beta_0}{1-\varepsilon}<\infty.$$ For each integer $r\ge3$, let $$\label{eq:quenched-correlation} C_{\varepsilon,\omega}(r)=\lim_{n\to\infty,\,n\ge r} \langle\sigma_0\sigma_{(r,0)}\rangle^{\mathrm f} _{n,\beta_c(\varepsilon),\omega}, \qquad R_\varepsilon(r)=\frac{\mathbb E[C_{\varepsilon,\omega}(r)^2]} {\mathbb E[C_{\varepsilon,\omega}(r)]^2}.$$ The free correlation limit exists by monotonicity in the volume and lies in $(0,1]$. In particular $R_\varepsilon(r)$ is defined and is at least one. All unqualified expectations $\mathbb E$ below are over the environment; auxiliary independent random choices used in the proof will be included explicitly when introduced.
- **quenched correlation and relative second moment** ([source](#eq:quenched-correlation)): The physical critical inverse temperature is $$\label{eq:critical-definition} m_\varepsilon(\beta)=\mathbb E\!\left[\lim_{n\to\infty} \langle\sigma_0\rangle^+_{n,\beta,\omega}\right], \qquad \beta_c(\varepsilon)=\inf\{\beta\ge0:m_\varepsilon(\beta)>0\}.$$ Ferromagnetic monotonicity gives the plus limit. Comparison with constant bonds, with $\beta_0=\tfrac12\log(1+\sqrt2)$, gives $$0<\frac{\beta_0}{1+\varepsilon}\le\beta_c(\varepsilon) \le\frac{\beta_0}{1-\varepsilon}<\infty.$$ For each integer $r\ge3$, let $$\label{eq:quenched-correlation} C_{\varepsilon,\omega}(r)=\lim_{n\to\infty,\,n\ge r} \langle\sigma_0\sigma_{(r,0)}\rangle^{\mathrm f} _{n,\beta_c(\varepsilon),\omega}, \qquad R_\varepsilon(r)=\frac{\mathbb E[C_{\varepsilon,\omega}(r)^2]} {\mathbb E[C_{\varepsilon,\omega}(r)]^2}.$$ The free correlation limit exists by monotonicity in the volume and lies in $(0,1]$. In particular $R_\varepsilon(r)$ is defined and is at least one. All unqualified expectations $\mathbb E$ below are over the environment; auxiliary independent random choices used in the proof will be included explicitly when introduced.
- **energy field and spin dimension** ([source](#ref:energy)): $$\label{ref:energy} e(z)=\frac14\sum_{v:\,\|v-z\|=1} \bigl(\sigma_z\sigma_v-\langle\sigma_z\sigma_v\rangle_0\bigr), \qquad d=\frac18.$$ We use Euclidean distances for field asymptotics and max-norm squares for conditional averaging. This distinction changes no buffer condition.
- **disk response** ([source](#ref:disk-response)): $$\label{ref:disk-response} d_R(F)=\langle F\rangle^+_{\mathrm{disk}(4R)}-\langle F\rangle_0.$$ For each sufficiently large fixed ratio $R/w$, and all sufficiently large $w$, the reference estimate gives $$\begin{aligned} \|d_R(F)\|&\le C(w/R)^b\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty, \label{ref:response-input}\\ \left\lVert P_RF^\circ\right\rVert_\infty &\le C\|d_R(F)\|+C(w/R)^p\left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty, \label{ref:operator-input} \end{aligned}$$ where $$\label{ref:exponents} (b,p)= \begin{cases} (1,3),&F\text{ is even and invariant under right-angle rotations},\\ (1,2),&F\text{ is even},\\ (d,1+d),&F\text{ is odd}. \end{cases}$$ The constant is uniform over sufficiently large fixed ratios, whereas the fine-mesh threshold can depend on the ratio. Ratios converging to a fixed admissible value are allowed. The norm bound is uniform over all cutting configurations [[ReferenceIsing](#ref-ReferenceIsing), Proposition 4.1].
- **animal** ([source](#pm-h-12)): An *animal* is a nonempty finite connected set of cells.
- **principal slot** ([source](#pm-h-12)): The index $p_c$ is the *principal slot* at $c$.
- **scales** ([source](#block:scales)): $$r_j=r_0L^j,\qquad r=r_j,\qquad R=Lr. \label{block:scales}$$ Partition $\mathbb Z^2$ into disjoint squares of side $r$, with centers in $r\mathbb Z^2$, and denote this collection by $\mathcal G_r$. These partitions are nested, and the origin is a cell center at every scale. Cells are adjacent when their closures meet, so corner adjacency is allowed. An *animal* is a nonempty finite connected set of cells. Write $\operatorname{par}(c)$ for the unique $R$-cell containing the $r$-cell $c$. Distances between cells, when expressed without units, are maximum-norm cell distances. Physical distances carry the factor $r$ or $R$ explicitly.
- **source jet** ([source](#block:jet)): $$H(\zeta)=\sum_i h_i+ \sum_{\varnothing\ne J\subset\{1,\ldots,m\}} \zeta_J\sum_i u_i^J, \qquad \zeta_J=\prod_{a\in J}\zeta_a. \label{block:jet}$$ A coefficient carrying $J$ must have a carrier containing the cells of all the corresponding marks. A one-mark list is anchored at its mark cell and has a marked principal slot there. For more than one mark we use animal slots and anchor at the first mark, in the prescribed order of labels. Source coefficients are not used in threshold decisions. The nilpotent variables record derivatives and products exactly; they place no positivity assumption on the source coefficients. We always use finitely many labels and source lists with finite weighted indexed norms, defined below.
- **projective tensor norm** ([source](#block:projective)): The function space at a slot is the finite-dimensional space of functions of its available spins, with its sup norm. For a tensor in $k$ such spaces we use the projective norm $$\\|F\\|_\pi= \inf\left\{\sum_{\alpha}\prod_{b=1}^k \\|f_{b,\alpha}\\|_\infty: F=\sum_{\alpha}f_{1,\alpha}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{k,\alpha} \right\}. \label{block:projective}$$ The infimum is over finite tensor representations, or their norm completion. In particular, $\\|f_1\otimes\cdots\otimes f_k\\|_\pi\le\prod_b\\|f_b\\|_\infty$. A bounded multilinear operation on the component function spaces extends to this tensor product with the same operator bound. Expectations of random tensors are taken componentwise; the inequality $\\|\mathbb EF\\|_\pi\le\mathbb E\\|F\\|_\pi$ then applies.
- **nonprincipal tail hypothesis** ([source](#block:tail-hypothesis)): The deterministic hypothesis on the nonprincipal background is $$\\|h_{A,c}\\|_\infty\le\delta e^{-w\|A\|}. \label{block:tail-hypothesis}$$ There is no such bound on principal slots. At a fixed finite-volume step they are finite real functions, with a possibly large deterministic bound. No stochastic moment hypothesis is used in this section.
- **normalised tilted conditional operator** ([source](#block:tilt)): For any spin function $F$, the normalized tilted conditional operator is $$P_Q^B F=\frac{P_Q(e^BF)}{P_Qe^B}. \label{block:tilt}$$ It is defined for every real bounded $B$, sends $1$ to $1$, and obeys $\\|P_Q^B F\\|_\infty\le\\|F\\|_\infty$. These properties require no smallness or ferromagnetic property of $B$. The operator still uses only reference conditional probabilities. The denominator in <a href="#block:tilt" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="block:tilt">[block:tilt]</a>, and its logarithm already saved above, are the normalization that will make the ensuing partition identity exact.
- **variance density coordinate** ([source](#lin:g)): Let $\ell$ and $\ell_s$ be the charges of Lemma <a href="#ref:charges" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ref:charges">2</a>. For an even function and a scaled odd function, respectively, put $$q_r(F)=r^{-1}\ell(F),\qquad q_{r,s}(U)=r^{-d}\ell_s(U).$$ These are bounded functionals on a slot of length $n$, with norms at most $C(n+S)$ and $C(n+S)^d$. For a homogeneous mean array $m$ and a homogeneous connected two-tensor $K$, define $$\begin{aligned} x_r(m)&=\sum_{a(i)=c}q_r(m_i),\label{lin:x}\\ g_r(K)&=\sum_{a(i)=c}\sum_{k\in \mathcal I_r} (q_r\otimes q_r)(K_{i,k}).\label{lin:g} \end{aligned}$$ The values do not depend on the cell $c$. The second sum converges absolutely: connectivity puts the second anchor within $C(n_i+n_k+S)r$ of the first, and exponential weights dominate this number of placements. The factor $r^{-2}$ in <a href="#lin:g" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lin:g">[lin:g]</a> divides the charge covariance by the number of microscopic sites in one cell. Thus $g_r$ is a variance density.
- **scaled marked charge** ([source](#mark:charge)): $$\label{mark:charge} \alpha_j=\frac1{r_j^d}\sum_i \ell_s(u_{j,i}),\qquad A_{j,q}=\mathbb E\alpha_j^q.$$ The initial insertion is $r_0^d\sigma_y$, so $\alpha_0=1$. The deterministic representative $w_{j,y}$ from Proposition <a href="#lin:marked" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lin:marked">18</a> has normalized spin charge one. All estimates below are uniform in $y$, including its position within a grid cell. Let $\mathcal Q$ be the finite set of even source powers fixed in Section <a href="#sec:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norms">4</a>, containing $2,4,400$. It was chosen before the background moment exponent $p$ and the blocking ratio $L$. We prove the moment asymptotics for every $q\in\mathcal Q$.
- **comparison sequence** ([source](#flow:comparison-sequence)): $$\label{flow:comparison-sequence} B_0=4\pi a^2\log L,\qquad G_j=\frac{\bar g}{1+B_0j\bar g},\qquad s_j=G_j^{1/2}.$$ In particular $G_{j+1}/G_j=(1+B_0G_j)^{-1}$ tends uniformly to one as $D$ tends to zero. A trajectory is *kept through scale $N$* if $\|x_j\|\le XG_j$ for $0\le j\le N$, where $X$ will be fixed below. An estimate for its tested next step concerns the actual output at $N+1$ without requiring that output to satisfy the thermal bound.
- **stopping scale** ([source](#read:stop)): Let $y_1,y_2\in\mathbb Z^2$ and put $\ell=\lVert y_1-y_2\rVert_\infty$. Fix $F$ larger than all principal support and dependence halos and all contact distances in one blocking step. Increase it, if necessary, so that the same separation holds in output-scale units for every step considered below. For sufficiently large $\ell$, let $J$ be the largest integer such that $$F\le \ell/r_J<LF. \label{read:stop}$$ Thus $J=\log_L\ell+O(1)$. None of our estimates requires the ratio $\ell/r_J$ to converge, or either mark to occupy a prescribed position inside its grid cell.

| q-main-exponent | limit of log relative second moment over log log distance | = | $\frac14$ | Fixed small disorder strength; distance tends to infinity |

| q-wired-arm-moment | high moment of wired arm probability | ≤ | $n^{-400/8+o(1)}$ | Box radius n large, uniform in the centre |
| q-threshold-derivative | derivative of averaged event probability | ≥ | $c\log\ell\, f_\ell(t)(1-f_\ell(t))$ | Compact parameter window |
| q-subcritical-arm | averaged wired arm probability below zero | ≤ | $C_{t',D}n^{-6}$ | Fixed negative parameter, small fixed D |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-invariant-charges** [claim](#stmt-ref-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ref-operator): Iterate nested conditional averaging on lines of reference fields, split into a bounded line and a contracting quotient, and take the limiting coefficient. -> invariant energy and spin charges
- **step-averaging-decay** [claim](#stmt-ref-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ref-operator): Use the same contraction on the quotient to obtain ordinary and improved decay. -> averaging and charge cancellation bounds
- **step-ope-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-ref-ope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ref-ope): Cut in separated squares, extract scale-normalised charges from separated limits, and compare with continuum product expansions. -> energy and energy-spin coefficients
- **step-buffered-comparison** [claim](#stmt-ref-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ref-comparison): Apply the reference conditional comparison to the inner law and integrate against a positive weight. -> bounded normalised weights
- **step-finite-dictionary** [claim](#stmt-ref-dictionary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ref-dictionary): Represent the outer-configuration density by finitely many weights and cover the coefficient ball by small sets. -> finite tests for averaged sup norm
- **step-carriers** [claim](#stmt-block-carrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-carrier): Cover a spanning-tree walk of the animal by pieces of length comparable to the block ratio. -> carrier size bound
- **step-tree-expansion** [claim](#stmt-block-tree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-tree): Polymer expansion with a spanning-tree bound on Mayer coefficients and rooted animal counting. -> tree coefficient bound and summability
- **step-exact-identity** [claim](#stmt-block-exact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-exact): Tower property over selected squares for each expansion term, then formal logarithm. -> exact partition identity
- **step-large-footprints** [claim](#stmt-block-diameter) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-diameter): Rerun the tree bound with doubled output weight and count nonchart footprints. -> exponential gain for large footprints
- **step-tail-preservation** [claim](#stmt-block-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-tails): Classify outputs as higher order or long singleton and bound each with reserved weight. -> preserved tail bound
- **step-finite-volume** [claim](#stmt-block-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-block-finite): Charts and gauge covariance on tori, boundary-clearance rules in boxes, finite anchor counting and induction. -> finite-volume transformation and bulk agreement
- **step-taylor-transport** [claim](#stmt-norm-taylor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-taylor): Compare actual and Taylor coefficients off bad events, bound bad events by Hölder before summing. -> Taylor transport of low cumulants
- **step-tilt-attenuation** [claim](#stmt-norm-attenuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-attenuation): Pointwise comparison to an intermediate square, then even-function decay and the tower property. -> attenuation of positive tilts
- **step-moment-reproduction** [claim](#stmt-norm-reproduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-reproduction): Bunch counting with interpolation and Young inequality for high moments; finite dictionary for the second moment. -> reproduced moment norms
- **step-source-estimates** [claim](#stmt-norm-source) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-source): Weighted Hölder in source-weighted measures and a positive linear majorant for the source map. -> source Taylor and mixed reproduction
- **step-background-linear** [claim](#stmt-lin-background) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lin-background): Subtract charges using reference lines and use improved decay on short inputs. -> contraction off distinguished directions
- **step-marked-linear** [claim](#stmt-lin-marked) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lin-marked): Rank-one decomposition of each slot with small remainder, extended to tensors. -> marked linear splitting
- **step-formal-profiles** [claim](#stmt-coef-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-profiles): Nested averaging for linear profiles and contracting recursions for higher cumulants. -> bounded formal profiles
- **step-insertion-invariance** [claim](#stmt-coef-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-invariance): Fix an outer contour, apply the exact identity inside and take disk-response limits. -> scale-independent charge coefficients
- **step-average-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-coef-averages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-averages): Compare linear and invariant charges, reduce to lattice sums of products, and evaluate the logarithmic sums. -> logarithmic coefficient sums
- **step-bounded-first** [claim](#stmt-coef-bounded-first) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-bounded-first): The quadratic invariant coefficient for one source vanishes exactly, so the increments telescope. -> bounded first-charge sums
- **step-finite-prefix** [claim](#stmt-flow-prefix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flow-prefix): Cumulant recursions, convolution with contraction, backward control of the thermal component, reciprocal summation. -> finite-prefix bounds and variance recursion
- **step-tuned-trajectory** [claim](#stmt-flow-tuned) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flow-tuned): Continuity in the initial mean coordinate and nested compact intervals of kept parameters. -> tuned parameter
- **step-variance-decay** [claim](#stmt-flow-variance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flow-variance): Sum the reciprocal recursion along the tuned trajectory. -> inverse-linear variance decay
- **step-marked-kernel** [claim](#stmt-mark-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mark-kernel): Simultaneous induction on charge moment, kernel component and mixed bound using contraction. -> marked list control
- **step-marked-connected** [claim](#stmt-mark-connected-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mark-connected-comparison): Connected cumulant recursions with contraction and comparison with the formal profile. -> multiplicative recurrence for charge moments
- **step-marked-moments** [claim](#stmt-mark-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mark-moments): Take logarithms of the recurrence and use Abel summation with the Cesàro means. -> moment asymptotics
- **step-first-moment** [claim](#stmt-mark-first) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mark-first): Variation of constants using bounded partial sums and the second moment bound. -> bounded first moment
- **step-joint-merge** [claim](#stmt-read-merge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-read-merge): Retain one background factor, control decision events by mixed moments, and use exponential gain from separated marks. -> small joint-source terms
- **step-readout-moments** [claim](#stmt-read-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-read-moments): Exact normalised identity, independence of principal amplitudes, buffer removal and reference conditional estimate. -> disorder moments at tuning
- **step-wired-arm** [claim](#stmt-read-arm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-read-arm): Edwards-Sokal representation, finite-box blocking away from the wall and deterministic bound for wall-touching terms. -> wired arm moment bound
- **step-torus-duality** [claim](#stmt-crit-torus-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-torus-duality): Euler-type identity for cycle spaces and weight ratio computation. -> homology duality and density comparison
- **step-torus-transfer** [claim](#stmt-crit-torus-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-torus-transfer): Compare twisted and untwisted runs of the finite-volume map in charts and bound nonchart scalars. -> torus nondegeneracy at tuning
- **step-threshold-inequality** [claim](#stmt-crit-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-threshold): Monotone sequential sampler, influence bounds by covariances and the product-space influence inequality. -> logarithmic derivative inequality
- **step-tuning-zero** [claim](#stmt-crit-tuning-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-tuning-zero): Self-duality at zero gives non-extreme character probabilities; integrate the logit inequality to a contradiction. -> tuned parameter equals zero
- **step-subcritical-arm** [claim](#stmt-crit-subcritical-arm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-subcritical-arm): Randomised shell exploration with revealment bounded on a good environment, then the monotone decision-tree inequality. -> polynomial arm decay below zero
- **step-magnetization-transition** [claim](#stmt-crit-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-identification): Zero magnetization from the arm bound; positivity from planar duality, crossings at several scales and stationarity. -> magnetization threshold at zero
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-crit-identification-2): Match physical couplings to tuned coordinates, then read off the ratio of moments at the stopping scale. -> logarithmic growth exponent

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-torus-homology-rank**: For every edge configuration A on the square torus, the rank of the homology image of the open cycle space of A and the rank for the complementary dual configuration add up to two.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-fk-weights-equal**: Reading the duality lemma as an equality of unnormalized random-cluster weights between a configuration and its dual. -> The lemma only compares normalized laws up to a bounded density; the unnormalized weights differ by a configuration-independent factor, so absolute weights are not preserved.
- **anti-finite-check-as-theorem**: Treating the exhaustive small-torus homology check as evidence for the asymptotic second-moment theorem. -> The finite check validates only a combinatorial identity; the main limit relies on deterministic reference estimates and a multiscale argument that are not exercised.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The torus duality identity is a finite combinatorial statement: for an explicit edge subset of a finite torus the homology rank of the configuration and of its dual can be computed by a finite procedure and checked against an independent component-count identity. The main limit theorem is asymptotic and not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: one line "L mask": torus side L (integer at least 3) and an integer bitmask of open edges (horizontal edges first, then vertical)
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= mask < 2^(2*L*L); edge index y*L+x is the horizontal edge from (x,y) to (x+1,y); L*L+y*L+x is the vertical edge from (x,y) to (x,y+1)
# OUTPUTS: one line "r rstar" with the rank of the homology image of A and of its complementary dual configuration
# TIME: O(L^2) union-find operations
# SPACE: O(L^2)
# ARITHMETIC: exact small integers; homology classes are 2-bit vectors combined by XOR
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and the process exits nonzero
import sys

def rank(classes):
    s = set(c for c in classes if c != 0)
    if not s:
        return 0
    return 2 if len(s) >= 2 else 1

def hom_rank(n, edges):
    parent = list(range(n))
    par = [0] * n
    def find(a):
        p = 0
        while parent[a] != a:
            p ^= par[a]
            a = parent[a]
        return a, p
    cyc = []
    for (u, v, c) in edges:
        ru, pu = find(u)
        rv, pv = find(v)
        if ru == rv:
            cyc.append(pu ^ pv ^ c)
        else:
            parent[ru] = rv
            par[ru] = pu ^ pv ^ c
    return rank(cyc)

def main():
    L, mask = [int(t) for t in sys.stdin.read().split()]
    n = L * L
    prim = []
    dual = []
    for y in range(L):
        for x in range(L):
            i = y * L + x
            u = i
            hv = y * L + (x + 1) % L
            vv = ((y + 1) % L) * L + x
            if (mask >> i) & 1:
                c = 1 if x == L - 1 else 0
                prim.append((u, hv, c))
            else:
                dc = 2 if y == 0 else 0
                dual.append((((y - 1) % L) * L + x, u, dc))
            j = n + i
            if (mask >> j) & 1:
                c = 2 if y == L - 1 else 0
                prim.append((u, vv, c))
            else:
                dc = 1 if x == 0 else 0
                dual.append((y * L + (x - 1) % L, u, dc))
    print(hom_rank(n, prim), hom_rank(n, dual))

main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys\n\ndef comps(n, edges):\n    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]\n    for u, v in edges:\n        adj[u].append(v)\n        adj[v].append(u)\n    seen = [0] * n\n    k = 0\n    for s in range(n):\n        if not seen[s]:\n            k += 1\n            seen[s] = k\n            st = [s]\n            while st:\n                a = st.pop()\n                for b in adj[a]:\n                    if not seen[b]:\n                        seen[b] = k\n                        st.append(b)\n    return k\n\ndef main():\n    inp = open(sys.argv[1]).read().split()\n    out = open(sys.argv[2]).read().split()\n    exp = sys.argv[3].split()\n    L, mask = int(inp[0]), int(inp[1])\n    n = L * L\n    A = []\n    D = []\n    for y in range(L):\n        for x in range(L):\n            i = y * L + x\n            if (mask >> i) & 1:\n                A.append((i, y * L + (x + 1) % L))\n            else:\n                D.append((((y - 1) % L) * L + x, i))\n            if (mask >> (n + i)) & 1:\n                A.append((i, ((y + 1) % L) * L + x))\n            else:\n                D.append((y * L + (x - 1) % L, i))\n    kA = comps(n, A)\n    kD = comps(n, D)\n    r = len(A) - n + kA - kD + 1\n    rs = len(D) - n + kD - kA + 1\n    if r + rs != 2:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    mine = [str(r), str(rs)]\n    if out != mine:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    if out != exp:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    sys.exit(0)\n\nmain()\n",
    "output_contract": "stdout: a single line \"r rstar\" of two integers in {0,1,2} whose sum is 2.",
    "reason": "The torus duality identity is a finite combinatorial statement: for an explicit edge subset of a finite torus the homology rank of the configuration and of its dual can be computed by a finite procedure and checked against an independent component-count identity. The main limit theorem is asymptotic and not covered.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: one line \"L mask\": torus side L (integer at least 3) and an integer bitmask of open edges (horizontal edges first, then vertical)\n# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= mask < 2^(2*L*L); edge index y*L+x is the horizontal edge from (x,y) to (x+1,y); L*L+y*L+x is the vertical edge from (x,y) to (x,y+1)\n# OUTPUTS: one line \"r rstar\" with the rank of the homology image of A and of its complementary dual configuration\n# TIME: O(L^2) union-find operations\n# SPACE: O(L^2)\n# ARITHMETIC: exact small integers; homology classes are 2-bit vectors combined by XOR\n# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and the process exits nonzero\nimport sys\n\ndef rank(classes):\n    s = set(c for c in classes if c != 0)\n    if not s:\n        return 0\n    return 2 if len(s) >= 2 else 1\n\ndef hom_rank(n, edges):\n    parent = list(range(n))\n    par = [0] * n\n    def find(a):\n        p = 0\n        while parent[a] != a:\n            p ^= par[a]\n            a = parent[a]\n        return a, p\n    cyc = []\n    for (u, v, c) in edges:\n        ru, pu = find(u)\n        rv, pv = find(v)\n        if ru == rv:\n            cyc.append(pu ^ pv ^ c)\n        else:\n            parent[ru] = rv\n            par[ru] = pu ^ pv ^ c\n    return rank(cyc)\n\ndef main():\n    L, mask = [int(t) for t in sys.stdin.read().split()]\n    n = L * L\n    prim = []\n    dual = []\n    for y in range(L):\n        for x in range(L):\n            i = y * L + x\n            u = i\n            hv = y * L + (x + 1) % L\n            vv = ((y + 1) % L) * L + x\n            if (mask >> i) & 1:\n                c = 1 if x == L - 1 else 0\n                prim.append((u, hv, c))\n            else:\n                dc = 2 if y == 0 else 0\n                dual.append((((y - 1) % L) * L + x, u, dc))\n            j = n + i\n            if (mask >> j) & 1:\n                c = 2 if y == L - 1 else 0\n                prim.append((u, vv, c))\n            else:\n                dc = 1 if x == 0 else 0\n                dual.append((y * L + (x - 1) % L, u, dc))\n    print(hom_rank(n, prim), hom_rank(n, dual))\n\nmain()\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "0 2",
        "input": "3 0"
      },

        "input": "3 262143"
      },

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an asymptotic statement about a disordered lattice model under assumed deterministic reference estimates. It specifies no program, interface or routine that could be built from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a limit law for quenched correlations. It gives no simulation scheme or sampler. The only fixed reference point is that the clean model has trivial relative second moment. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is a hand-built multiscale argument resting on cited deterministic estimates. It yields no checkable tool or verifier for general inputs. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The blocking map is defined for infinite or arbitrarily large systems and via formal logarithms and limits. It is not a finite procedure with a stated cost, so no pseudocode or complexity is claimed. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Lattice animals and slot lists are proof bookkeeping devices for counting connected expansions. The paper does not propose them as an implementable data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardware. Its only computational remark is that the coefficient calculation needs finite-degree polynomial algebra, which is not an accelerator design. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no adversary, key, hardness assumption or randomness-extraction claim. Disorder is a physical environment, not a security primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, data set, loss or generalisation statement appears. The quenched-average structure is a statistical-mechanics object. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tuning of the parameter t is an existence argument via nested intervals and continuity, not an optimisation procedure with objective, constraints or guarantees. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independence of far-apart insertions is a probabilistic property of the disorder, not a protocol, consensus or communication statement. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of this result is mathematical physics, as a rigorous statement of disordered critical behaviour with a physical inverse temperature, not an engineering artefact. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-reference-estimates-assumed** (hypothesis): The main theorem is conditional: it assumes the stated deterministic critical-reference estimates at the scopes of the reference section. It does not prove them, and it supplies neither a random critical point nor a quenched moment estimate by itself. Blocked use: ['Quoting the logarithmic exponent as an unconditional theorem', 'Applying the result when the deterministic reference estimates are not available at the required scopes']
- **limit-small-disorder-threshold** (hypothesis): The exponent one quarter is asserted only for fixed disorder strengths in an interval below a threshold whose size is not made explicit. Blocked use: ['Using the exponent for disorder strengths outside the small interval', 'Taking the disorder strength to zero together with the distance']
- **limit-order-of-limits** (edge_case): The result concerns a specific order of limits: disorder strength fixed first, then the free thermodynamic limit for each separation, then the disorder moments and the distance limit. Other orders are not covered. Blocked use: ['Reading finite-box or simultaneous-limit numerics as a test of the exponent', 'Exchanging the disorder average with the thermodynamic limit']
- **limit-constants-depend-on-disorder** (approximation): Constants and error terms depend on the fixed disorder strength, so the asymptotic law gives no rate or explicit size of corrections uniform in that strength. Blocked use: ['Extracting explicit finite-distance error bars from the asymptotic law', 'Claiming uniformity of the error terms over the allowed disorder interval']
- **limit-no-uniform-in-D** (formal_scope): In the readout stage no large-distance asymptotic that is uniform as the tuned disorder coordinate tends to zero is asserted. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the large-distance limit with vanishing disorder', 'Using the readout moment asymptotics as a joint limit in distance and disorder']
- **limit-principal-fields-unbounded** (edge_case): Principal slots carry no deterministic bound in the exact blocking step. The construction handles large principal fields only through positive conditional weights and normalised operators, so estimates must not be run through exponentials of principal norms. Blocked use: ['Applying small-field cluster bounds directly to principal slots', 'Assuming a uniform deterministic bound on principal fields in one step']
- **limit-tuned-parameter-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The tuned temperature parameter is obtained by a continuity and nested-interval argument. It is not computed by any procedure, and only its order in the disorder strength is stated. Blocked use: ['Using the construction as an algorithm that outputs the tuned parameter', 'Claiming an explicit value or convergence rate for the shooting argument']
- **limit-little-o-meaning** (approximation): In the moment estimates the small-o notation denotes a fixed positive power gain at fixed constants, not a limit uniform in the moment constants. Blocked use: ['Treating the small-o error as uniform in the moment constants', 'Optimising the constants while keeping the small-o term fixed']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Assuming the stated deterministic critical-reference estimates, we prove that the relative second moment of the quenched critical spin correlation in the square-lattice Ising model with independent fair bonds $`1\pm\varepsilon`$ grows as $`(\log r)^{1/4+o(1)}`$ for each sufficiently small fixed <i>ε</i> &gt; 0. The correlation is evaluated at the physical critical temperature, with the thermodynamic limit taken before the disorder moments and the large-distance limit.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Weak random bonds in the planar Ising model provide a basic example in which disorder is expected to change critical correlations through logarithmic factors. The fluctuations of a quenched correlation are particularly revealing: the correlation is first computed in a fixed environment, with its own Gibbs normalization, and only then averaged over the bonds. This paper establishes the predicted logarithmic growth of its relative second moment for small, fixed binary disorder.
