**Proposition 23** (Finite-prefix estimates). *There are fixed constants $X,C$ and $\rho<1$ such that, for sufficiently small $D>0$, every trajectory kept through scale $N$ satisfies the background tail and moment bounds of Section <a href="#sec:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norms">4</a> with $s=s_j$, and $$\label{flow:rough}
 \left\lVert m_j\right\rVert+\left\lVert K_j\right\rVert\le CG_j,\qquad
 \left\lVert W_j\right\rVert+\left\lVert Y_j\right\rVert\le CG_j^2,\qquad
 \tfrac12G_j<g_j<2G_j .$$ The tensor and moment bounds also hold for the tested next step, with a larger fixed constant for its mean. Moreover, for $1\le j\le N$, $$\begin{aligned}
 K_j-g_jV_j&=O\bigl(G_j(G_j+\rho^j)\bigr),\label{flow:cov-profile}\\
 m_j-g_jm_j^*&=O\bigl(G_j(G_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})\bigr),
 \label{flow:mean-profile}\\
 W_j-g_j^2W_j^*,\quad Y_j-g_j^2Y_j^*
 &=O\bigl(G_j^2(s_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})\bigr).
 \label{flow:higher-profile}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $V_j,m_j^*,W_j^*,Y_j^*$ are the bounded formal profiles of Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">6</a>. For $0\le j\le N$, including the tested next covariance, $$\label{flow:variance-recursion}
 g_{j+1}=g_j+b_jg_j^2+
 O\bigl(G_j^2(s_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})\bigr).$$ The constants are independent of $N$ and of the kept trajectory.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-flow-prefix"><summary>Proof of stmt-flow-prefix</summary>

*Proof.* We first derive the estimates under the corresponding bounds on an input prefix, and then choose the constants so that the estimates reproduce those bounds. This separation is useful because no sign condition is available for an individual $b_j$.

<a id="flow:connected-partitions"></a>
The finite Taylor rule in Proposition <a href="#norm:taylor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:taylor">13</a> determines the cumulant recursion with remainder $O(G_j^{5/2})$. The required algebra can be described explicitly. Assign a separate letter to every factor in a Taylor monomial, even when two letters have the same slot index. If $I_1,\ldots,I_k$ are the groups belonging to the $k$ output factors, the cumulant of their products is $$\label{flow:connected-partitions}
 \mathop{\mathrm{cum}}\left(\prod_{a\in I_1}h_a,\ldots,
                 \prod_{a\in I_k}h_a\right)
 =\sum_{\pi:\,\pi\vee\{I_1,\ldots,I_k\}=\mathbf 1}
       \ \prod_{B\in\pi}\mathop{\mathrm{cum}}(h_a:a\in B).$$ The join condition says precisely that the partition connects the output groups. Formula <a href="#flow:connected-partitions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:connected-partitions">[flow:connected-partitions]</a> follows by expanding moments into cumulants and applying the defining alternating sum for the output cumulant. The coefficient of a partition not connecting the groups is zero. It therefore applies also to correlated input slots.

<a id="flow:mean-recursion"></a>
<a id="flow:cov-recursion"></a>
<a id="flow:third-recursion"></a>
<a id="flow:fourth-recursion"></a>
A singleton block costs $m=O(G)$, a pair costs $K=O(G)$, and blocks of sizes three and four cost $W,Y=O(G^2)$, where $G=G_j$. For covariance the nonlinear degree patterns with at most four letters are $(1,2),(2,1),(1,3),(2,2),(3,1)$. The three-letter patterns retain $W$ and the connecting $mK$ partitions; the four-letter patterns retain $Y$ and the connecting $KK$ partitions. For the third cumulant the only further pattern has four letters and retains $Y$ and $KK$. For the fourth cumulant there is only homogeneous linear transport to the stated accuracy. The mean retains its linear term and the quadratic Taylor coefficient applied to $K$; its other terms have size $O(G^2)$. In particular the recursions have the form $$\begin{aligned}
 m_{j+1}&=T_jm_j+Q_j(K_j)+O(G_j^2),\label{flow:mean-recursion}\\
 K_{j+1}&=T_j^{(2)}K_j+
       \mathcal B_j(W_j,Y_j,K_j\otimes K_j,m_j\otimes K_j)
       +O(G_j^{5/2}),\label{flow:cov-recursion}\\
 W_{j+1}&=T_j^{(3)}W_j+
       \mathcal F_j(Y_j,K_j\otimes K_j)+O(G_j^{5/2}),
       \label{flow:third-recursion}\\
 Y_{j+1}&=T_j^{(4)}Y_j+O(G_j^{5/2}).\label{flow:fourth-recursion}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $Q_j$ is the quadratic Taylor coefficient contracted against a covariance; $\mathcal B_j$ and $\mathcal F_j$ denote the sums of exactly the connected partitions just listed, including their Taylor coefficients. They are bounded linear maps on the displayed direct sums of tensor spaces. The indexed estimates in Proposition <a href="#norm:taylor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:taylor">13</a> justify all slot sums.

<a id="flow:convolution"></a>
The charge of linear covariance transport is unchanged by Proposition <a href="#lin:background" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lin:background">17</a>. Equations <a href="#flow:rough" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:rough">[flow:rough]</a> and <a href="#flow:cov-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:cov-recursion">[flow:cov-recursion]</a> therefore first give the coarse estimate $g_{j+1}-g_j=O(G_j^2)$. On the kernel of that charge the linear operator contracts. Subtracting $g_{j+1}V_{j+1}$, and using the exact transport of $V_j$, gives <a href="#flow:cov-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:cov-profile">[flow:cov-profile]</a> by convolution with this contraction. For clarity, every such convolution uses the elementary estimate $$\label{flow:convolution}
 \sum_{i<j}\kappa^{j-1-i}G_i^\alpha
 \le C_\alpha G_j^\alpha,\qquad
 \kappa^jG_0^\alpha\le C_\alpha\rho^jG_j^\alpha,$$ for the finitely many positive exponents $\alpha$ used here, where $\kappa<\rho<1$. To verify it, use $G_i/G_j\le(1+B_0\bar g)^{j-i}$ and decrease $D$ so that $\kappa(1+B_0\bar g)^\alpha<\rho$.

Subtract next the recursion for $g_jm_j^*$ from <a href="#flow:mean-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:mean-recursion">[flow:mean-recursion]</a>. The stable mean component is controlled forward by <a href="#flow:convolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:convolution">[flow:convolution]</a>. Its forcing is $O(G_j^2+G_j\rho^j)$. The thermal component is controlled backward: if its error is $z_j$, then $$z_j=L^{-1}z_{j+1}+O(G_j^2+G_j\rho^j),\qquad |z_N|\le CG_N.$$ Summing this equality from $N$ backwards gives $|z_j|\le CG_j(G_j+\rho^j+\rho^{N-j})$, after enlarging $\rho<1$. This proves <a href="#flow:mean-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:mean-profile">[flow:mean-profile]</a>. The fourth and third cumulant recursions, in that order, have contracting homogeneous operators. Subtract their formal-profile recursions, use $g_{j+1}^2-g_j^2=O(G_j^3)$, and apply <a href="#flow:convolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:convolution">[flow:convolution]</a>. The resulting errors are those in <a href="#flow:higher-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:higher-profile">[flow:higher-profile]</a>. An unmatched initial profile contributes only the geometric term. All comparisons at scale zero can likewise be paid by that term.

The definition of $b_j$ is the charge of the nonlinear term in <a href="#flow:cov-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:cov-recursion">[flow:cov-recursion]</a> evaluated on the formal profiles. Inserting <a href="#flow:cov-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:cov-profile">[flow:cov-profile]</a>–<a href="#flow:higher-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:higher-profile">[flow:higher-profile]</a> therefore gives <a href="#flow:variance-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:variance-recursion">[flow:variance-recursion]</a>. The terminal term in that formula is the cost of using only a finite thermal prefix.

<a id="flow:reciprocal"></a>
<a id="flow:uniform-ratio"></a>
We now show why this refined estimate reproduces the asserted covariance range. The coarse one-step bound first makes the tested $g_{N+1}$ positive and comparable to $G_{N+1}$, so reciprocals are legitimate. Taking reciprocals in <a href="#flow:variance-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:variance-recursion">[flow:variance-recursion]</a> and summing gives, for $1\le k\le N+1$, $$\label{flow:reciprocal}
 \frac1{g_k}=\frac1{g_0}-\sum_{j<k}b_j+
 O\left(1+\sum_{j<k}s_j\right).$$ Indeed $g_j/G_j$ is comparable to one on the input prefix, the two geometric errors have bounded sums, and the reciprocal Taylor remainder is $O(G_j)$, bounded by $O(s_j)$. Proposition <a href="#coef:averages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="coef:averages">21</a> says that $R_k=\sum_{j<k}(b_j+B_0)=o(k)$. Thus for each $\eta>0$ there is $C_\eta<\infty$ such that $|R_k|\le\eta k+C_\eta$ for every $k$. Since $s_j\le\sqrt{\bar g}$, <a id="flow:relative-reciprocal"></a>$$\label{flow:relative-reciprocal}
 \sup_{1\le k\le N+1}
 \frac{\left|g_k^{-1}-(\bar g^{-1}+B_0k)\right|}
      {\bar g^{-1}+B_0k}
 \le C D+C\sqrt{\bar g}+C\eta+C_\eta\bar g.$$ First choosing $\eta$ small and then $D$ small makes this strictly smaller than the amount allowed by $G_k/2<g_k<2G_k$. It also proves that $$\label{flow:uniform-ratio}
 \sup_{k\le N+1}|g_k/G_k-1|\longrightarrow0\quad(D\downarrow0),$$ uniformly over all kept prefixes. Only the averaged coefficient was used; individual coefficients may have either sign.

<a id="flow:outward"></a>
It remains to fix the constants in the bounds used above. First choose the covariance-kernel allowance larger than its initial and line contributions. Contraction reproduces this allowance, with its nonlinear $O(G^2)$ loss left for the final smallness choice. Choose $X$ so large that, under this covariance bound, $$\label{flow:outward}
 x_{j+1}=Lx_j+f_j,\qquad |f_j|\le C G_j,
 \qquad (L-1)X>C+1.$$ Choose the remaining mean allowance from this tube, the initial mean, and stable contraction. Next choose the fourth-cumulant allowance and then the third-cumulant allowance, using <a href="#flow:fourth-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:fourth-recursion">[flow:fourth-recursion]</a> and <a href="#flow:third-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:third-recursion">[flow:third-recursion]</a>. These choices furnish a fixed bound $F_*$ on the actual next principal raw second tensor. Indeed, at each principal slot $i$, $$\left\lVert\mathbb E[h'_i\otimes h'_i]\right\rVert
 =\left\lVert K'_{i,i}+m'_i\otimes m'_i\right\rVert
 \le C G_j+C G_j^2\le e^{-2v}F_*^2G_j,$$ after enlarging $F_*$ by the fixed factor $e^v$ if necessary. This is precisely the tensor input required by the moment reproduction estimate; no output probability norm has been assumed to obtain it.

For the moment bounds in Proposition <a href="#norm:reproduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:reproduction">15</a>, choose $B_2$ larger than the fixed multiple of $F_*$ and the initial second moment bound, and $B$ larger than the initial high-moment bound and $C_L^*B_2$, with strict room in both inequalities. The dictionary error was chosen to have $\varepsilon_* C_L^*$ sufficiently small, so these choices can also satisfy $\varepsilon_*B\ll B_2$. Finally decrease $D$ to pay all nonlinear remainders, the variation of $G_j$, and all fixed constants in the profile comparisons. The tail bound is reproduced by the exact map. This order closes the input-prefix induction and its tested next step. In particular, the covariance improvement uses only the previously kept prefix, not an as yet unproved next thermal bound. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-flow-tuned"></a>
<div id="flow:tuned" class="proposition">

**Proposition 24** (Existence of a tuned trajectory). *For every sufficiently small $D>0$ there is $t_*(D)=O(D^2)$ whose background trajectory satisfies $|x_j|\le XG_j$ at every scale. All the bounds of Proposition <a href="#flow:prefix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flow:prefix">23</a> hold along this trajectory. In the profile estimates its terminal geometric term may be omitted.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-flow-tuned"><summary>Proof of stmt-flow-tuned</summary>

*Proof.* At every fixed scale the expected list coordinates are continuous in $x_0$. Indeed the map has finite random footprints; each fresh threshold has a continuous distribution, so equality with that threshold has probability zero. Almost-sure continuity away from those equalities, together with the finite-step moment bounds and summable coefficient bounds, gives dominated convergence. This applies uniformly on each compact parameter interval used below.

Choose the initial interval $I_0$ so that its endpoints have $x_0=-XG_0$ and $x_0=XG_0$. Suppose $I_j$ is a closed interval all of whose parameters are kept through $j$, and whose endpoints have $x_j=-XG_j$ and $x_j=XG_j$. By <a href="#flow:outward" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:outward">[flow:outward]</a>, their next coordinates lie strictly below $-XG_{j+1}$ and above $XG_{j+1}$, respectively. Continuity supplies a subinterval $I_{j+1}\subset I_j$ on which $|x_{j+1}|\le XG_{j+1}$ and whose endpoints attain both bounds. For an explicit choice, take the first hitting of the upper bound and the last preceding hitting of the lower bound. Between those points neither bound can be crossed. The nested nonempty compact intervals have a common parameter, giving the asserted trajectory. Finally, apply the finite-prefix profile bounds to arbitrarily long prefixes of this trajectory, with $j$ fixed, and let $N$ tend to infinity. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-flow-variance"></a>
<div id="flow:variance" class="corollary">

**Corollary 25** (Decay of the disorder coordinate). *Along every tuned trajectory obtained above, <a id="flow:variance-asymptotic"></a>$$\label{flow:variance-asymptotic}
 g_j\sim\frac1{B_0j}\quad(j\to\infty),\qquad
 \sum_{j\ge0}|g_{j+1}-g_j|\le C\bar g.$$ The uniform comparison <a href="#flow:uniform-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:uniform-ratio">[flow:uniform-ratio]</a> also holds over all $j\ge0$ as $D\downarrow0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-flow-variance"><summary>Proof of stmt-flow-variance</summary>

*Proof.* In <a href="#flow:reciprocal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:reciprocal">[flow:reciprocal]</a>, $R_j=o(j)$ and $\sum_{i<j}s_i=o(j)$ for each fixed $D>0$, because $s_i\to0$. Thus $g_j^{-1}=B_0j+o(j)$. The coarse increment estimate and $\sum_jG_j^2\le C\bar g$ give the total-variation bound. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:marked"></a>
### Transport of a spin insertion

Fix a tuned background from Proposition <a href="#flow:tuned" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flow:tuned">24</a>. A singleton spin insertion at $y$ is transported by the exact marked map, with the factor $L^d$ included at every step. Denote its scaled random list by $u_j=u_{j,y}$, and set <a id="mark:charge"></a>$$\label{mark:charge}
 \alpha_j=\frac1{r_j^d}\sum_i \ell_s(u_{j,i}),\qquad
 A_{j,q}=\mathbb E\alpha_j^q.$$ The initial insertion is $r_0^d\sigma_y$, so $\alpha_0=1$. The deterministic representative $w_{j,y}$ from Proposition <a href="#lin:marked" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lin:marked">18</a> has normalized spin charge one. All estimates below are uniform in $y$, including its position within a grid cell. Let $\mathcal Q$ be the finite set of even source powers fixed in Section <a href="#sec:norms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:norms">4</a>, containing $2,4,400$. It was chosen before the background moment exponent $p$ and the blocking ratio $L$. We prove the moment asymptotics for every $q\in\mathcal Q$.

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
#### Control of the random marked list

For a random marked list, write $\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{v,q}$ for its weighted individual-slot $L^q$ norm, with the weights of Proposition <a href="#norm:source" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:source">16</a>. The source-background bound is the mixed bound there; its exponent $p$ is fixed sufficiently large relative to $q$. The following statement supplies both that bound and the small component perpendicular to spin charge.

<a id="stmt-mark-kernel"></a>
<div id="mark:kernel" class="proposition">

<a id="mark:lq"></a>
<a id="mark:mixed"></a>
<a id="mark:kernel-small"></a>
**Proposition 26**. *For each $q\in\mathcal Q$ and sufficiently small $D>0$, $A_{j,q}>0$ and there are fixed constants $C_*,B_s,C$ such that, at every scale, $$\begin{aligned}
 \left\lVert u_j\right\rVert_{v,q}&\le C_*A_{j,q}^{1/q},\label{mark:lq}\\
 \bigl(\mathbb E\left\lVert u_{j,i}\right\rVert^q\left\lVert h_{j,m}\right\rVert^p\bigr)^{1/p}
 &\le (C_*A_{j,q}^{1/q})^{q/p}B_s s_j
       e^{-vq n_i/p-vn_m}.\label{mark:mixed}
\end{aligned}$$ For $j\ge1$ one has the stronger estimate $$\label{mark:kernel-small}
 \left\lVert u_j-\alpha_jw_{j,y}\right\rVert_{v,q}
       \le C A_{j,q}^{1/q}s_j.$$ The notation $\alpha_jw_{j,y}$ places the representative in its marked principal slot. The allowed disorder can be chosen uniformly for the fixed finite collection $\mathcal Q$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mark-kernel"><summary>Proof of stmt-mark-kernel</summary>

<a id="mark:rough-charge"></a>
*Proof.* Put $a_j=A_{j,q}^{1/q}=\left\lVert\alpha_j\right\rVert_{L^q}$ and $k_j=\left\lVert u_j-\alpha_jw_{j,y}\right\rVert_{v,q}$. The source-line projection is bounded in the weighted norm. Its complement contracts under the scaled linear transport, and Proposition <a href="#norm:source" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:source">16</a> bounds the nonlinear difference by $CUs_j$ when $U=C_*a_j$. Since linear transport preserves normalized spin charge, $$\label{mark:rough-charge}
 |a_{j+1}-a_j|\le C C_*a_js_j,
 \qquad
 k_{j+1}\le\kappa k_j+C C_*a_js_j,$$ where the first inequality is the reverse triangle inequality in $L^q$. In particular $a_{j+1}>0$ and $a_{j+1}/a_j=1+O(s_j)$ once disorder is small. This proves positivity before any normalization by the next moment is used.

To close <a href="#mark:lq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:lq">[mark:lq]</a>, reserve a fixed part of $C_*a_j$ for the line and the rest for the kernel. Choose $C_*$ large enough for the bounded line norm and the initial list. The strict contraction in the second inequality of <a href="#mark:rough-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:rough-charge">[mark:rough-charge]</a> then reproduces the kernel allowance relative to $a_{j+1}$, after decreasing $D$. The mixed output estimate in Proposition <a href="#norm:source" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:source">16</a> is $$U^{q/p}\bigl(B_s s_j/8+C_{L,B}s_j+o(s_j)\bigr).$$ Choose $B_s$ after the background constants so that its first two terms leave strict room below $B_s s_j$, and then decrease $D$ so that the remaining room pays the changes from $U$ to $C_*a_{j+1}$ and from $s_j$ to $s_{j+1}$. Initially the source is deterministic, so the mixed bound follows directly from the background moment bound. This closes the simultaneous induction for <a href="#mark:lq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:lq">[mark:lq]</a>–<a href="#mark:mixed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:mixed">[mark:mixed]</a>.

At zero background the first step gives exactly the representative $w_{1,y}$. Its perturbation is $O(a_0s_0)$, hence $k_1\le Ca_1s_1$. Divide the kernel recursion in <a href="#mark:rough-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:rough-charge">[mark:rough-charge]</a> by $a_{j+1}s_{j+1}$. The ratios $a_j/a_{j+1}=1+O(s_j)$ and $s_j/s_{j+1}=1+O(G_j)$ make its contraction coefficient strictly smaller than one. Iteration proves <a href="#mark:kernel-small" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:kernel-small">[mark:kernel-small]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-34"></a>
#### Connected moments and the scalar recurrence

<a id="mark:connected-objects"></a>
Fix $q\in\mathcal Q$ and abbreviate $A_j=A_{j,q}$. Use separate slot indices for all tensor factors and define $$\label{mark:connected-objects}
 S_j=\mathbb E[u_j^{\otimes q}],\qquad
 C_{1,j}=\mathop{\mathrm{cum}}(u_j^{\otimes q},h_j),\qquad
 C_{2,j}=\mathop{\mathrm{cum}}(u_j^{\otimes q},h_j,h_j).$$ In each cumulant $u_j^{\otimes q}$ is *one random tensor-valued argument*; the $q$ marked factors are not separate cumulant groups. Taking the product of their normalized spin charges gives the scalar coordinate $A_j$ of $S_j$. All products and cumulants in <a href="#mark:connected-objects" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:connected-objects">[mark:connected-objects]</a> are therefore defined entry by entry before summing their indexed projective norms.

<a id="stmt-mark-connected-comparison"></a>
<div id="mark:connected-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="mark:c2"></a>
<a id="mark:c1"></a>
<a id="mark:s-profile"></a>
<a id="mark:scalar-recursion"></a>
**Lemma 27**. *For $j\ge1$, with the bounded formal profile $Z_{j,q,y}^*$ from Section <a href="#sec:coefficients" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coefficients">6</a>, $$\begin{aligned}
 C_{2,j}&=O(A_js_j^3),\label{mark:c2}\\
 C_{1,j}&=A_jg_j Z_{j,q,y}^*
       +O\bigl(A_jG_j(s_j+\rho^j)\bigr),\label{mark:c1}\\
 S_j&=A_jw_{j,y}^{\otimes q}+O(A_js_j).\label{mark:s-profile}
\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the marked charge moments obey $$\label{mark:scalar-recursion}
 A_{j+1}=A_j\left(1+c_{j,q}(y)g_j+
                    O\bigl(G_j(s_j+\rho^j)\bigr)\right).$$ The recurrence also holds at $j=0$, where its error is allowed to be $O(G_0)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mark-connected-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-mark-connected-comparison</summary>

<a id="mark:varying-convolution"></a>
*Proof.* The mixed estimates give initially $\left\lVert C_{k,j}\right\rVert\le CA_js_j^k$ for $k=1,2$. Apply the source Taylor rule through two background degrees, counting the displayed background arguments among those degrees. For $C_2$ the only retained term is homogeneous linear transport: a nonlinear term in either background output has at least three background letters, and a nonlinear term in the transported source tensor also adds a third letter. The degree-zero transport of the grouped source tensor is deterministic and linear on that tensor. Thus no independent $S_jK_j$ term is present in this cumulant recursion. By Proposition <a href="#lin:marked" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lin:marked">18</a>, <a id="mark:c2-recur"></a>$$\label{mark:c2-recur}
 C_{2,j+1}=\mathcal L_{2,j}C_{2,j}+O(A_js_j^3),
 \qquad \left\lVert\mathcal L_{2,j}\right\rVert\le\kappa.$$ The initial grouped source tensor is deterministic, so $C_{2,0}=0$. Contraction now proves <a href="#mark:c2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:c2">[mark:c2]</a>. More explicitly, for each of the finitely many exponents $b$ below, $$\label{mark:varying-convolution}
 \sum_{i<j}\kappa^{j-1-i}A_i s_i^b\le C_b A_j s_j^b.$$ Indeed <a href="#mark:rough-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:rough-charge">[mark:rough-charge]</a> gives $A_i/A_j\le(1+C\sqrt{\bar g})^{j-i}$, and $s_i/s_j\le(1+B_0\bar g)^{(j-i)/2}$; their product can be absorbed in the strict contraction by choosing $D$ small.

<a id="mark:c1-recur"></a>
The corresponding expansion of $C_1$ has its homogeneous term and terms with two background letters. To see their structure without any assumption on the disorder law, set $U=u^{\otimes q}$ in the moment-cumulant identity. For example, $$\mathbb E[U\otimes h\otimes h]
 =C_2+S\otimes K+S\otimes m\otimes m
       +C_1\otimes m+m\otimes C_1,$$ with the indicated permutations of indices. The source-linear, background-quadratic term and the source-background term covaried with one background are obtained from this identity by subtracting their products of expectations. Their only possible leading inhomogeneous contribution is a bounded bilinear operation on $S,K$; all the $C_2$ and $C_1m$ terms are $O(A_js_j^3)$ at this stage. Consequently $$\label{mark:c1-recur}
 C_{1,j+1}=\mathcal L_{1,j}C_{1,j}
       +\mathcal H_j(S_j,K_j)+O(A_js_j^3),
 \qquad\left\lVert\mathcal L_{1,j}\right\rVert\le\kappa,$$ where $\mathcal H_j$ is exactly the sum of these degree-two inhomogeneous Taylor terms. Since $C_{1,0}=0$, contraction first gives $C_{1,j}=O(A_jG_j)$.

<a id="mark:improved-ratio"></a>
In the expansion of $S_{j+1}$, the degree-one terms now use $C_{1,j}$ and $S_jm_j$, and the degree-two terms use $S_jK_j$; all other terms cost $O(A_js_j^3)$. Since $m_j,K_j=O(G_j)$, taking product spin charge improves <a href="#mark:rough-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:rough-charge">[mark:rough-charge]</a> to $$\label{mark:improved-ratio}
 A_{j+1}/A_j=1+O(G_j).$$ To identify its coefficient, expand $u_j=\alpha_jw_{j,y}+(u_j-\alpha_jw_{j,y})$ in the $q$ tensor factors. Every term except the all-line term contains a kernel factor. Hölder, <a href="#mark:lq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:lq">[mark:lq]</a>, and <a href="#mark:kernel-small" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:kernel-small">[mark:kernel-small]</a> bound its expectation by $CA_js_j$, proving <a href="#mark:s-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:s-profile">[mark:s-profile]</a>.

The formal profile satisfies $$Z_{j+1,q,y}^*=\mathcal L_{1,j}Z_{j,q,y}^*
                   +\mathcal H_j(w_{j,y}^{\otimes q},V_j).$$ Subtract this equation multiplied by $A_{j+1}g_{j+1}$ from <a href="#mark:c1-recur" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:c1-recur">[mark:c1-recur]</a>. The replacements of $S_j$ and $K_j$ by their line parts cost $O(A_jG_j(s_j+\rho^j))$, by <a href="#mark:s-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:s-profile">[mark:s-profile]</a> and <a href="#flow:cov-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:cov-profile">[flow:cov-profile]</a>. The change of scalar factors costs $O(A_jG_j^2)$, by <a href="#mark:improved-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:improved-ratio">[mark:improved-ratio]</a> and $g_{j+1}-g_j=O(G_j^2)$. Contraction and <a href="#mark:varying-convolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:varying-convolution">[mark:varying-convolution]</a> prove <a href="#mark:c1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:c1">[mark:c1]</a>. Finally insert the three profile comparisons and <a href="#flow:mean-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:mean-profile">[flow:mean-profile]</a>, with its terminal term removed, into the Taylor formula for the spin charge of $S_{j+1}$. Its leading coefficient is exactly $c_{j,q}(y)$ by definition. The accumulated error is that in <a href="#mark:scalar-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:scalar-recursion">[mark:scalar-recursion]</a>. Scale zero has bounded coefficients and errors of order $A_0G_0$, which the geometric term allows. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
#### Summation of the moments

<a id="stmt-mark-moments"></a>
<div id="mark:moments" class="proposition">

<a id="mark:asymptotic"></a>
<a id="mark:second-uniform"></a>
**Proposition 28**. *For each $q\in\mathcal Q$ and fixed sufficiently small $D>0$, $$\label{mark:asymptotic}
 A_{j,q}=j^{\binom q2/8+o(1)}\qquad(j\to\infty),$$ uniformly in the marked site $y$. In addition there is a constant independent of sufficiently small $D$ such that $$\label{mark:second-uniform}
 A_{j,2}\le C(1+B_0j\bar g)^{1/4}\qquad(j\ge0).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mark-moments"><summary>Proof of stmt-mark-moments</summary>

<a id="mark:log-sum"></a>
*Proof.* The error in <a href="#mark:scalar-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:scalar-recursion">[mark:scalar-recursion]</a> has summable absolute values, because $$\sum_{j\ge0}G_j(s_j+\rho^j)<\infty,\qquad
 \sum_{j\ge0}g_j^2<\infty.$$ All factors are positive and uniformly close to one for small $D$. Taking logarithms therefore gives $$\label{mark:log-sum}
 \log A_{J,q}=\sum_{j<J}c_{j,q}(y)g_j+O_D(1).$$ The error is uniform in $y$. Let $\mu_q=\binom q2 B_0/8$. Proposition <a href="#coef:averages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="coef:averages">21</a> gives $\sum_{j<J}(c_{j,q}(y)-\mu_q)=o(J)$ uniformly in $y$. Abel summation then gives $\sum_{1\le j<J}(c_{j,q}(y)-\mu_q)/j=o(\log J)$: for each $\eta>0$, the partial sums are at most $\eta j+C_\eta$, and the summation-by-parts remainder is at most $\eta\log J+O_\eta(1)$. The coefficients are bounded, so replacing $1/(B_0j)$ by $g_j=(1+o(1))/(B_0j)$ costs another $o(\log J)$. This proves <a href="#mark:asymptotic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:asymptotic">[mark:asymptotic]</a> from <a href="#mark:log-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:log-sum">[mark:log-sum]</a>.

For the bound uniform in $D$, write $c_j=c_{j,2}(y)$ and $\mu=B_0/8$. Uniform Cesaro convergence gives $|\sum_{i<k}(c_i-\mu)|\le\eta k+C_\eta$. Since $G_j$ decreases, summation by parts implies <a id="mark:uniform-abel"></a>$$\label{mark:uniform-abel}
 \sum_{j<J}c_jG_j
 \le(\mu+\eta)\sum_{j<J}G_j+C_\eta\bar g
 \le\left(\frac18+\frac\eta{B_0}\right)
        \log(1+B_0J\bar g)+C_\eta.$$ By <a href="#flow:uniform-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flow:uniform-ratio">[flow:uniform-ratio]</a>, replacing $G_j$ by $g_j$ costs at most $\left\lVert c\right\rVert_{\infty}\sup_j|g_j/G_j-1|\sum_{j<J}G_j$. Choose $\eta$ and then the disorder bound so that the total coefficient of the logarithm is less than $1/4$. The sums of the Taylor errors and of $g_j^2$ are uniformly bounded (in fact they tend to zero with $D$). Applying the logarithmic estimate proves <a href="#mark:second-uniform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:second-uniform">[mark:second-uniform]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
#### The first moment

The first charge moment need not be used as a norm. Instead, the second moment estimate supplies an absolute bound for its Taylor remainders. This avoids presuming either its sign or its comparability to one.

<a id="stmt-mark-first"></a>
<div id="mark:first" class="proposition">

**Proposition 29**. *Uniformly in $j$ and $y$, for sufficiently small $D>0$, <a id="mark:first-bound"></a>$$\label{mark:first-bound}
 \frac1C\le A_{j,1}=\mathbb E\alpha_j\le C.$$ More precisely, $\sup_{j,y}|A_{j,1}-1|\to0$ as $D\downarrow0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mark-first"><summary>Proof of stmt-mark-first</summary>

<a id="mark:first-recursion"></a>
*Proof.* Set $U_j^*=C(1+B_0j\bar g)^{1/8}$. Equations <a href="#mark:lq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:lq">[mark:lq]</a>, <a href="#mark:mixed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:mixed">[mark:mixed]</a>, and <a href="#mark:second-uniform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:second-uniform">[mark:second-uniform]</a>, with $q=2$, give all one-source absolute estimates with $A_j$ in the previous proof replaced by $U_j^*$. To retain the full background smallness in this passage, write $Y=\left\lVert u_{j,i}\right\rVert$ and $H=\prod_m\left\lVert h_{j,m}\right\rVert^{a_m}$, with $b=\sum_m a_m\le p$. Cauchy–Schwarz gives $$\mathbb E[YH]\le\bigl(\mathbb E[Y^2H]\bigr)^{1/2}
               \bigl(\mathbb E[H]\bigr)^{1/2}
 \le C U_j^*s_j^b e^{-vn_i-v\sum_m a_mn_m}.$$ The first factor uses the mixed $q=2$ measure and the second the background moment bound. Unused powers in both measures pay any large-field indicator in the Taylor remainder as in Proposition <a href="#norm:source" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:source">16</a>. Apply the same connected-cumulant recursions to the single grouped source $u_j$. They yield $$C_{2,j}=O(U_j^*s_j^3),\quad C_{1,j}=O(U_j^*G_j),\quad
 S_j=A_{j,1}w_{j,y}+O(U_j^*s_j).$$ The change of its scalar coordinate is bounded absolutely by $|A_{j+1,1}-A_{j,1}|\le CU_j^*G_j$. When comparing the $C_1$ recursion with its formal profile, use this absolute difference bound in place of <a href="#mark:improved-ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:improved-ratio">[mark:improved-ratio]</a>. Together with $|A_{j,1}|\le U_j^*$ it bounds the change of $A_{j,1}g_j$ by $CU_j^*G_j^2$. The same contracting convolution proves $$C_{1,j}=A_{j,1}g_jZ_{j,1,y}^*
               +O\bigl(U_j^*G_j(s_j+\rho^j)\bigr).$$ Consequently, with $a_j=A_{j,1}$, $$\label{mark:first-recursion}
 a_{j+1}=(1+c_{j,1}(y)g_j)a_j+e_j,
 \qquad |e_j|\le CU_j^*G_j(s_j+\rho^j).$$

Corollary <a href="#coef:bounded-first" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="coef:bounded-first">22</a> gives a fixed bound on every partial sum of $c_{j,1}(y)$, uniformly in $y$. Abel summation and Corollary <a href="#flow:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flow:variance">25</a> therefore imply $$\sup_J\left|\sum_{j<J}c_{j,1}(y)g_j\right|
 \le C\left(g_0+\sum_{j\ge0}|g_{j+1}-g_j|\right)
 \le C\bar g.$$ Also $\sum_jc_{j,1}(y)^2g_j^2\le C\bar g$. Thus the products $P_J=\prod_{j<J}(1+c_{j,1}(y)g_j)$ are positive and satisfy $\sup_{J,y}|P_J-1|\le C\bar g$. The inhomogeneous errors have total cost $$\begin{aligned}
 \sum_{j\ge0}|e_j|
 &\le C\bar g^{3/2}\sum_{j\ge0}
             (1+B_0j\bar g)^{-11/8}
       +C\bar g\sum_{j\ge0}\rho^j
             (1+B_0j\bar g)^{-7/8}\\
 &\le C\sqrt{\bar g}.
\end{aligned}$$ The exponent $11/8>1$ is what makes the first sum integrable on the scale $j\asymp\bar g^{-1}$. Variation of constants in <a href="#mark:first-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:first-recursion">[mark:first-recursion]</a>, with $a_0=1$, gives $$a_J=P_J\left(1+\sum_{j<J}e_j/P_{j+1}\right).$$ It follows that $\sup_{J,y}|a_J-1|\le C\sqrt{\bar g}$, which proves both assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:readout"></a>
### Quenched partition readout and boundary arms

The marked estimates concern random functions in a partition integrand. We now recover the spin correlation normalized separately in each random environment. Two features of this passage matter. The charge of an entire source list need not depend on local randomness, so independence will be used only after replacing it by the charge of the principal slot. Also, large terminal background interactions cannot be discarded globally. We remove only the interactions meeting a growing buffer around the two marks and use a conditional reference estimate inside that buffer.

Throughout this section $D>0$ is fixed and sufficiently small, and $t=t_*(D)$ is the tuned parameter of Proposition <a href="#flow:tuned" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="flow:tuned">24</a>. The expectation $\mathbb E$ includes the independent decision seeds of the blocking construction. All assertions about growth as the scale index tends to infinity are uniform in the locations of the marks. Constants may depend on $D$ and on the fixed geometric parameters. In particular, no uniform large-distance asymptotic as $D\downarrow0$ is asserted.

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
#### The exact identity and the two local amplitudes

<a id="read:stop"></a>
Let $y_1,y_2\in\mathbb Z^2$ and put $\ell=\lVert y_1-y_2\rVert_\infty$. Fix $F$ larger than all principal support and dependence halos and all contact distances in one blocking step. Increase it, if necessary, so that the same separation holds in output-scale units for every step considered below. For sufficiently large $\ell$, let $J$ be the largest integer such that $$F\le \ell/r_J<LF.
 \label{read:stop}$$ Thus $J=\log_L\ell+O(1)$. None of our estimates requires the ratio $\ell/r_J$ to converge, or either mark to occupy a prescribed position inside its grid cell.

<a id="read:jet"></a>
Use two commuting square-free variables $\zeta_1,\zeta_2$, so $\zeta_1^2=\zeta_2^2=0$. Start the log integrand with $\zeta_1\sigma_{y_1}+\zeta_2\sigma_{y_2}$; its initial joint coefficient is zero. At scale $j$ write the source-dependent part of the transformed log integrand and its saved scalar as $$r_j^{-d}\sum_{a=1}^2\zeta_a\bigl(U_{j,a}+k_{j,a}\bigr)
 +r_j^{-2d}\zeta_1\zeta_2\bigl(U_{j,12}+k_{j,12}\bigr),
 \qquad d=\tfrac18.
 \label{read:jet}$$ Here $U_{j,a}=\sum_i u_{j,a,i}$ is the sum of the scaled singleton list, $U_{j,12}$ is the sum of the scaled joint list, and $k_{j,a},k_{j,12}$ are the accumulated scalar coefficients with the indicated scaling. Saved scalars retain their carriers until all estimates involving those carriers have been made. The individual singleton lists coincide with those obtained by running one source alone: set the other square-free variable equal to zero and then differentiate in the chosen variable.

<a id="read:exact-normalized"></a>
We apply the exact blocking identity first in a free box $\Lambda_N$ containing the marks and all relevant buffers. Write $\langle\cdot\rangle_{J,N,\omega}$ for expectation in its actual terminal background: the reference measure multiplied by the transformed positive weight and divided by its own source-zero partition integral. The saved source-zero scalar cancels in this ratio, however large it is. Use the plane centering convention also for source coefficients near the free boundary. Spin flip then makes $k_{J,1}=k_{J,2}=0$. Expanding the exponential of <a href="#read:jet" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:jet">[read:jet]</a> therefore gives the exact identity $$r_J^{2d}\langle\sigma_{y_1}\sigma_{y_2}\rangle^{\mathrm{free}}_{N,\omega}
 =\langle U_{J,1}U_{J,2}+U_{J,12}\rangle_{J,N,\omega}+k_{J,12}.
 \label{read:exact-normalized}$$ This is an identity at each environment and each choice of decision seeds. In particular it computes the quenched correlation and not a ratio of disorder-averaged partition integrals. For every function $Q$, the bound $|\langle Q\rangle_{J,N,\omega}|\le\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_\infty$ is independent of the terminal interactions outside its support. This is the reason that the sup-norm errors below remain usable after readout.

<a id="read:principal-replacement"></a>
We next estimate the plane lists. Their use in <a href="#read:exact-normalized" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:exact-normalized">[read:exact-normalized]</a> will be justified by the fixed-step finite-volume passage below. Let $u_{j,y,\mathrm{pr}}$ denote the principal slot of the singleton list at $y$. In addition to its total normalized charge $\alpha_{j,y}$ from Section <a href="#sec:marked" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:marked">8</a>, define <a id="read:local-charge"></a>$$\widetilde\alpha_{j,y}
       =r_j^{-d}\ell_s(u_{j,y,\mathrm{pr}}),
 \qquad
 w_{j,y}=r_j^d P_{Hr_j,c_j(y)}\sigma_y.
 \label{read:local-charge}$$ The line representative $w_{j,y}$ occupies the principal slot, has normalized charge one, and has uniformly bounded sup norm. The charge bound of Lemma <a href="#ref:charges" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ref:charges">2</a>, with exponential weights paying all carrier-size factors, shows that the charge functional is bounded on a singleton list. The nonprincipal slots of $u_{j,y}-\alpha_{j,y}w_{j,y}$ are exactly those of $u_{j,y}$. Consequently Proposition <a href="#mark:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mark:kernel">26</a> gives, for each of the fixed even powers $q$ used below and $j\ge1$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \left\lVert\alpha_{j,y}-\widetilde\alpha_{j,y}\right\rVert_{L^q}
   &\le C A_{j,q}(y)^{1/q}s_j,\\
 \left\lVert u_{j,y}-\widetilde\alpha_{j,y}w_{j,y}\right\rVert_{v,q}
   &\le C A_{j,q}(y)^{1/q}s_j,
 \label{read:principal-replacement}
\end{aligned}$$ where $A_{j,q}(y)=\mathbb E\alpha_{j,y}^q$ and the weighted norm includes slotwise function sup norms. Summation over slots preserves these bounds because the weight has the counting slack of Section <a href="#sec:blocking" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:blocking">3</a>.

<a id="read:product-moments"></a>
The random variable $\widetilde\alpha_{J,y}$ is measurable with respect to the environment and all decision seeds in the fixed principal footprint at $y$. Those footprints at $y_1,y_2$ are disjoint by the choice of $F$, including the safety layer for edge variables. Hence $\widetilde\alpha_{J,y_1}$ and $\widetilde\alpha_{J,y_2}$ are exactly independent. This assertion concerns the two amplitudes; it makes no independence assertion about the Gibbs state in which they will be read. Propositions <a href="#mark:moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mark:moments">28</a> and <a href="#mark:first" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mark:first">29</a>, together with <a href="#read:principal-replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:principal-replacement">[read:principal-replacement]</a>, imply, for large $J$, <a id="read:local-moments"></a>$$\begin{split}
  c\le \mathbb E\widetilde\alpha_{J,y}\le C,\\
  \left\lVert\widetilde\alpha_{J,y}\right\rVert_{L^2}
       =J^{1/16+o(1)},\\
  \left\lVert\widetilde\alpha_{J,y}\right\rVert_{L^4}
       =J^{3/16+o(1)}.
 \end{split}
 \label{read:local-moments}$$ For the first assertion the error is at most $C A_{J,2}^{1/2}s_J=o(1)$; for the other two, the error in $L^q$ is $o(A_{J,q}^{1/q})$. Thus, with $Z_J=\widetilde\alpha_{J,y_1}\widetilde\alpha_{J,y_2}$, $$c\le\mathbb EZ_J\le C,
 \qquad \mathbb E|Z_J|\le J^{1/8+o(1)},
 \qquad \left\lVert Z_J\right\rVert_{L^2}=J^{1/8+o(1)},
 \qquad \left\lVert Z_J\right\rVert_{L^4}=J^{3/8+o(1)}.
 \label{read:product-moments}$$ The mean factors even if the amplitudes are signed. The upper bound for $\mathbb E|Z_J|$ also follows from the $L^2$ bound.

<a id="stmt-read-merge"></a>
<div id="read:merge" class="lemma">

<a id="read:merge-bound"></a>
**Lemma 30** (Separated joint-source terms). *At the stopping scale <a href="#read:stop" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:stop">[read:stop]</a>, the sum of the sup norms of the joint-source list and the absolute values of its saved scalar increments, including their subsequent scaling, has $L^2$ norm at most $$C s_J J^{3/8+o(1)}=J^{-1/8+o(1)}.
 \label{read:merge-bound}$$ Replacing $U_{J,1}U_{J,2}$ by $Z_Jw_{J,y_1}w_{J,y_2}$ has the same $L^2$ bound on its sup norm.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-read-merge"><summary>Proof of stmt-read-merge</summary>

*Proof.* A newly generated coefficient containing both labels is bilinear in the two singleton lists. Apply the connected coefficient bounds of Lemma <a href="#block:tree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="block:tree">9</a>, retaining the output carrier containing both marks. At zero background degree a pair of principal slots does not merge: their input carriers, averaging squares, and contact neighborhoods are disjoint, also in output units, by our choice of $F$. Their transformed moment therefore factors and contributes zero to the joint logarithmic coefficient. At the first step the two input sources are principal. At later steps a zero-background merge must therefore contain a remainder from <a href="#read:principal-replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:principal-replacement">[read:principal-replacement]</a>; Hölder’s inequality in $L^4$ gives a factor $s_j$ in its $L^2$ cost.

For ordinary analytic terms of positive background degree, retain one background factor before bounding the others by the small thresholds and the deterministic tail bounds. To spell out why the retained factor is small despite dependence, suppress the slot weights and write $X_a=\left\lVert u_{j,a,i_a}\right\rVert$ and $H=\left\lVert h_m\right\rVert$. The mixed bound of Proposition <a href="#norm:source" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:source">16</a>, for source power four, and Hölder interpolation with respect to the measure $X_a^4\,\,\mathrm d\mathbb P$ give $$\mathbb E(X_a^4H^2)
 \le \bigl(\mathbb EX_a^4H^p\bigr)^{2/p}
       \bigl(\mathbb EX_a^4\bigr)^{1-2/p}
 \le C U_a^4s_j^2,
 \qquad U_a=C A_{j,4}(y_a)^{1/4}.$$ It follows that $$\left\lVert X_1X_2H\right\rVert_{L^2}^2
 \le\bigl(\mathbb EX_1^4H^2\bigr)^{1/2}
      \bigl(\mathbb EX_2^4H^2\bigr)^{1/2}
 \le C U_1^2U_2^2s_j^2.$$ The slot weights interpolate in the same way.

There is a separate estimate for changes caused by a large-field decision. The actual map and its ordinary analytic comparison agree unless one of the finitely many local badness tests in that term succeeds. For a tested principal slot $m$, let $B_m=\{\left\lVert h_m\right\rVert>\delta\}$; a random threshold in $[\delta,2\delta]$ can only reduce this event. The same mixed bound gives $$\mathbb E(X_a^4\mathbf 1_{B_m})
 \le\delta^{-p}\mathbb E(X_a^4\left\lVert h_m\right\rVert^p)
 \le C U_a^4s_j^p,
 \qquad
 \left\lVert X_1X_2\mathbf 1_{B_m}\right\rVert_{L^2}
 \le C U_1U_2s_j^{p/2}.$$ Normalized tilted moments, and their joint logarithmic derivatives, are bounded by a fixed multiple of $X_1X_2$; their bounds contain no exponential of a large principal norm. Thus the last display also bounds the decision-dependent terms. The number and positions of tested centers cost only the polynomial carrier factors already paid by the tree weights. Summing the tests and the analytic coefficients as in Lemma <a href="#block:tree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="block:tree">9</a> gives a fresh joint coefficient of cost at most $C U_1U_2s_j$, since $p/2>1$. All additional small factors and placements are paid by the thresholds and exponential slack. This separates the smallness supplied by a retained analytic factor from the smallness supplied by the rare decision event.

<a id="read:fresh-merge"></a>
An animal containing both marks has length at least $c\ell/r_{j+1}$. Retain one further fixed positive exponential weight on the output before summing it or saving a scalar. The fresh cost is therefore at most $$C A_{j,4}(y_1)^{1/4}A_{j,4}(y_2)^{1/4}s_j
       \exp(-c\ell/r_{j+1}).
 \label{read:fresh-merge}$$ The finitely many initial-scale adjustments obey this bound with a fixed constant. Once generated, a joint coefficient is transported linearly in that coefficient. The deterministic source bound, with normalized tilts and the fixed source rescaling, bounds its total weighted sup cost by a constant $K$ per step, uniformly in the sizes of the principal background fields. Scalars obey the same bound when their rescaling is included. Thus their accumulated cost is bounded by the sum of <a href="#read:fresh-merge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:fresh-merge">[read:fresh-merge]</a> multiplied by $K^{J-j-1}$, over $0\le j<J$.

For completeness, this sum is uniform over the whole stopping window. For $j\ge J/2$, $s_j\le C_Ds_J$ and $A_{j,4}(y_a)^{1/4}\le C_{D,\eta}J^{3/16+\eta}$ for every $\eta>0$. Writing $m=J-j-1$, the remaining numerical series is bounded by $$\sum_{m\ge0}K^m\exp(-cF L^m)<\infty.$$ For $j<J/2$, the same exponential contains a factor at most $\exp(-cF L^{J/2-1})$, which pays $K^J$ and any polynomial bound on the earlier marked moments. This proves <a href="#read:merge-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:merge-bound">[read:merge-bound]</a>, with arbitrarily small fixed power losses in place of its $o(1)$.

Finally set $R_a=U_{J,a}-\widetilde\alpha_{J,y_a}w_{J,y_a}$. The identity $$U_{J,1}U_{J,2}-Z_Jw_{J,y_1}w_{J,y_2}
 =R_1U_{J,2}+
   \widetilde\alpha_{J,y_1}w_{J,y_1}R_2$$ and <a href="#read:principal-replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:principal-replacement">[read:principal-replacement]</a> give the same $L^2$ bound by Hölder with fourth moments. No independence is used in this last estimate. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-39"></a>
#### The fixed-step passage from boxes to plane lists

We explain the finite-volume passage before using the stochastic plane bounds in <a href="#read:exact-normalized" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:exact-normalized">[read:exact-normalized]</a>. Fix the marks and the number $J$ of steps. Proposition <a href="#block:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="block:finite">12</a> bounds the principal functions deterministically at each such step, uniformly in $N$. For the fixed source labels, Lemma <a href="#block:tree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="block:tree">9</a> gives bounded weighted operator costs for transport and for each merge, also under the normalized bad-field averages. Starting from the bounded microscopic sources, iteration through these finitely many steps therefore bounds the source lists and their saved marked increments deterministically, uniformly in $N$; scalar carriers are retained when applying the weights. With any of the fixed exponential weights, the tails of the source lists and of background lists touching a fixed region are summable, with constants allowed to depend on $J$. Given a carrier cutoff, all remaining footprints and all their decision neighborhoods lie in a fixed finite set. Once $N$ contains that set, their coefficients equal the plane coefficients exactly under the common coupling of environment and seeds. First use this equality for the finitely many retained slots and increments, and then let the carrier cutoff increase. The deterministic tail bounds show that the corresponding errors tend to zero in every fixed $L^q$ used here. This argument also applies to the carrier-weighted marked scalar increments before they are summed. Odd local increments vanish by spin flip; in the free box the parity statement holds for the entire construction.

<a id="read:amplitude-readout"></a>
In this order of limits, Lemma <a href="#read:merge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="read:merge">30</a> and <a href="#read:principal-replacement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:principal-replacement">[read:principal-replacement]</a> imply $$r_J^{2d}\langle\sigma_{y_1}\sigma_{y_2}\rangle^{\mathrm{free}}_{N,\omega}
 =Z_J\langle w_{J,y_1}w_{J,y_2}\rangle_{J,N,\omega}
       +\mathcal R_{J,N},
 \quad
 \limsup_{N\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathcal R_{J,N}\right\rVert_{L^2}
       \le C s_JJ^{3/8+o(1)}.
 \label{read:amplitude-readout}$$ Here replacement of the finite-volume principal charges by the plane charges has zero error for all sufficiently large $N$. We do not need the terminal measures themselves to converge: a uniform estimate of the expectation in <a href="#read:amplitude-readout" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="read:amplitude-readout">[read:amplitude-readout]</a> will suffice.

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
#### Removing a buffer and taking the two disorder moments

The functions $w_{J,y_1},w_{J,y_2}$ are supported in a region of bounded diameter in $r_J$ units. Enclose it by a square buffer with margin <a id="read:buffer-radius"></a>$$b=J^{4/25}
 \label{read:buffer-radius}$$ terminal cells. Figure <a href="#read:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="read:geometry">2</a> shows the principal random footprints and the surrounding region used to compare the spin expectation with its reference value.

<figure id="read:geometry">

<figcaption>Geometry of the terminal readout. The solid inner squares are the reference contours defining $w_{J, ya}$, centered at the cells $c_{J}(y_{a})$. The dashed principal footprints record the disorder and decision seeds determining $α̃_{J, ya}$; their disjointness, including the safety layer for edge variables, gives independence of the two amplitudes. The dotted enclosure has bounded diameter in terminal cell units. A surrounding square provides margin of order $br_{J}$, where $b = J^{4/25}$. Removing terminal interactions whose spin supports meet this square leaves a reference conditional expectation inside it. The two spin observables are still evaluated jointly in that expectation. Shapes and relative lengths are schematic.</figcaption>
</figure>
